Semver: on this evidence the step is **minor** — every dropped prop is either unused, unstyled, or both, and every prop any meta in either corpus passes still works; Step 5 records the call for the whole exit. **RECORDED 2026-09-11: the owners chose MINOR for the whole exit** — `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md:265` ("THE SEMVER CALL IS MADE: the owners chose to ship the exit as a MINOR (2026-09-11)"), and step 5 reasons from it at line 846 ("a MINOR that removes published props has to be actionable"). The forward reference is discharged; the bump itself is still pending only because the release is deferred.

  **Gates.** The markup-independent behavioural layer was run BEFORE the snapshots were regenerated and was green on the new markup while the old baseline was still red: `examples.behavior-contract` + `UIRender.table-behavior` + `UIRender.listbox-behavior` + `UIRender.overlay-behavior`, 4 suites / 104 tests. Then one `-u`, then all three React legs at identical totals (158 suites / 2396 tests / 38 snapshots on 16.14, 17 and 18.3), per-file coverage (`Table.js` at 100/100/100/100, its own new threshold), `lint:js`, `lint:css`, `docs:views:check`, `docs:props:check`. The DOM zero-tripwires (`FIXED_PROP_LEAKS`, `FIXED_MARKUP_JUNK`) stayed at zero and the `name`-only-on-a-form-control invariant stayed at 57. **Still owed and not delivered by this step:** the mandatory Playwright visual/keyboard/a11y suite (§9.7-F1.3) does not exist in the repo, so the `.ui.table` padding contract is verified here by reading the compiled CSS and by class-string assertions, not by rendered pixels.

- **Step 2 — `TooltipPop` (S–M). SPLIT IN THREE, AND ALL THREE HAVE SHIPPED.** In-house tooltip: CSS placement + hover/focus triggers with delay + `aria-describedby`. Part 1 was the gate and the measurement, part 2 the browser leg, part 3 the replacement.

  **Why it was split.** The replacement could not be judged, because the component had no gate that a rewrite could fail. `TooltipPop.test.js` mocked the whole `semantic-ui-react` module and asserted the props object handed to the mock — assertions that pass against a `Popup` rendering nothing, describing the seam this step deletes. Layer 1 cannot see the component at all, and not merely because the corpus's two declarations sit in an inactive `Tabs` panel: a closed tooltip adds **nothing** to the document, so even an active one would leave all 38 snapshots unchanged. Part 1 was therefore measurement plus gate, product code untouched; part 2 is the replacement. Sequencing lesson worth reusing at step 3: measure and gate in a separate PR from the swap, because the measurement changed what the swap is *for*.

  **Part 1 — SHIPPED (tests and docs only).** 63 tooltip tests across four files where there were 10 — and only 5 of those 10 could fail if the tooltip broke, because the other 5 asserted the props handed to a mock:
  - `components/__tests__/TooltipPop.test.js` — **rewritten against the real `semantic-ui-react`**, no `jest.mock`. The emitted DOM, the exact class string with the CSS rule each token feeds, the portal's mount parent, the `title` shapes (string/number/element/function/`header`), the passthrough surface, and the a11y and prop-leak tripwires.
  - `components/__tests__/TooltipPop.behavior.test.js` — new. What opens and closes it, in milliseconds, as behaviour rather than markup.
  - `pages/main/__tests__/UIRender.overlay-behavior.test.js` — extended from one meta entry point to all three (`view: "Tooltip"`, a string `tooltip` attribute, an object `tooltip`), plus the click path and the `items` failure mode.
  - `style/__tests__/css.tooltip-contract.test.js` — new. Compiles the real CSS through the real webpack PostCSS stage, renders the real open tooltip, and runs `Element.matches()` rule by rule. This is the join neither the markup tests nor the CSS tests could see.

  **Four corrections to what this step believed.**
  1. It was not ungated: `UIRender.overlay-behavior.test.js` already drove the real component through the real engine for 5 clauses. What was missing was everything markup-shaped and every dismissal path — no class pin, no portal-location pin, no click, no Escape, no click-outside, no close delay, no object-`tooltip` passthrough, no prop-leak boundary.
  2. "The corpus renders zero tooltips" is true of **this** component only. The corpus renders **5**, all in the `slider` example and all snapshot-gated, from a *second* tooltip: `components/Tooltip.js`, 15 lines, CSS-only, an inline `<span>`, no portal and no JS positioning, used by `Slider`, `modules/upload/views/Upload.js` and the form validation-error tooltip, styled by 41 rules in `style/components/tooltip.less`. It is both evidence for the positioning decision and a naming trap for the cleanup — ~~`form/utils.js` imports **both**~~ **(evidence clause corrected 2026-09-17: it no longer does. `grep -n Tooltip src/core/modules/form/utils.js` returns exactly two hits — `:7 import ToolTip from '../../components/Tooltip'` and an unrelated `validationErrorsTooltip` at `:572`; the `TooltipPop` import went with the dead chain at `bbf7d607`. The naming duplication itself is still real and still the H6 rename item: `Tooltip.js` 15 lines and `TooltipPop.js` 516 lines coexist.)** **The duplication closed 2026-10-02 (§9.9-H6):** `TooltipPop.tsx` is `Tooltip.tsx`, and the CSS bubble that held the name is `TooltipBubble.tsx`.
  3. **"Keep emitting `ui popup`-compatible classNames until Step 4 so the current CSS continues to apply" rests on a false premise: no `.ui.popup` rule applies today.** The 13 declaration blocks that select this exact class string (plus 6 placement-keyed `:before` rules for the arrow) are all `.ui-render`-scoped by prefixwrap; `.ui-render` is a `<div>` and SUIR's `PortalInner` mounts into `document.body`, outside it. Measured twice — by selector matching in jsdom and by computed style in real Chrome, where the bubble comes out with `background: rgba(0,0,0,0)`, no border, no padding, `max-width: none`, `z-index: auto` and `::before { content: none }`, i.e. bare text overlapping the trigger. The class contract becomes real the moment the replacement mounts inside the widget, and SUIR's own `mountNode` already shows that it can.
  4. The largest coverage lesson: `TooltipPop.js` was already at **100% statements / branches / functions / lines** before part 1. Line coverage was never the gate and a per-file threshold would not have been one either.

  **Part 2 — SHIPPED (the browser leg).** 38 Playwright tests over Chromium (39 after part 3 added the out-of-flow limitation site): a corpus spec (every tooltip a meta can declare), a purpose-built harness page (`src/demo/pages/TooltipHarness.jsx`) for the cases the corpus cannot express, a `hasTouch` project, and a keyboard/a11y spec whose helpers step 3's `Dropdown` matrix reuses. Every expected value lives in `e2e/reference.js` tagged `[I]` invariant / `[R]` current behaviour / `[R->I]` pinned defect, so a value that must change when the component changes says so. That tagging is what made part 3 tractable: 23 of the 38 tests were `[R]`/`[R->I]` facts about the wrapper, and each had to be re-measured and rewritten rather than deleted.

  **Part 3 — SHIPPED (the replacement).** `TooltipPop.js` no longer imports `semantic-ui-react`. It renders `<span class="tooltip-host">` around the trigger plus a `Tooltip` bubble — the same 15-line inline `<span>` the product has shipped for years through `components/Tooltip.js` — mounted **only while open**, placed by CSS in `tooltip.less`. Mount-on-open is the crux rather than an implementation detail: a bubble that is in the DOM is revealed instantly by `*:hover > .tooltip` and no class defeats that at equal specificity, so mount-on-open is what buys the 500 ms delay, focus-open, Escape and click-outside at once.

  **It was a fix, not a trade.** Measured in real Chrome on the production build at every use site a meta can declare: the SUIR bubble rendered at the document origin — ~730 px from its trigger on `buttonIcon`, 2538–3006 px at the deep `all` site — with no `data-popper-placement` anywhere, and **every open raised an uncaught `TypeError`** from popper's flip modifier. Cause: SUIR clones the trigger with a `ref`, and of everything a meta can declare nothing can hold one (`mapper.js` uses the plain `Row`; `Button` is a `React.memo(function Button)`), so popper's reference element was `null` and `getClippingParents(null)` threw before a coordinate was written. On top of that the bubble portaled into `document.body`, outside `.ui-render`, so prefixwrap left all 13 `.ui.popup` rules unable to match and the live tooltip was unstyled text. There was no working positioning to lose. Measured now: 40 px centre-to-centre at **both** sites, painted, and nothing thrown.

  **Positioning primitive — DECIDED: (b), zero-dep, CSS off the host box.** `@floating-ui/dom` was not needed once the measurement showed there was no positional behaviour to match, and CSS anchor positioning stays rejected (not Baseline). The costs are named where they are measured, not summarised away: no flip, no viewport shift, no 250 px wrap, the bubble clipped by an `overflow: hidden` ancestor, and a trigger taken **out of normal flow** leaves the host collapsed so the bubble follows the host (its own harness section). The first three were reachable **only** on the harness page — from a meta, popper threw before writing a coordinate, so nothing in the product ever flipped. Step 3's option list therefore does **not** inherit a shared positioning primitive from this step; it makes its own call.

  **Decisions, all four discharged.** `omitProps` applied — `view`, `index` and `symbol` no longer reach the bubble as HTML attributes. `on` **dropped**, on the maintainers' instruction, and recorded in `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` under `dropped.on` since the CHANGELOG is step 5's debt — **a debt since PAID (step 5, `f7567ec3`; the changelog was brought true 2026-09-15): `src/demo/markdowns/changelog.md:268–279` carries the full entry, including the `on` drop, the touch-only consequence and the 45→13 narrowing of the object `tooltip` attribute. Residual, NOT yet fixed and worth a follow-up: `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md:174` and `:327` still say the CHANGELOG is step 5's debt, and that page ships in the npm tarball — fix at the curation source, `scripts/wrapper-prop-curation.js`, then re-run `npm run docs:props`** **(fixed 2026-10-02: both rows now name the changelog's entry, which points to them)**; focus-open, `role="tooltip"`, the bubble `id` and `aria-describedby` came in as a *consequence*, because with click gone and hover unavailable to a keyboard there would be no path to the content at all. Passthrough narrowed from **45 reachable names to 13**, with the 19 that were reachable-and-plausible warning once each — though only inside this repo, since the library is built in production mode only, so `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` is what actually reaches a consumer. Trigger shape: `React.Children.only` is gone with the portal, so the `items` form of `view: "Tooltip"` now renders instead of throwing the engine's diagnostic. The dead `TooltipPop` chain in `modules/form/utils.js` is deleted at all four sites, line 7 untouched.

  **Two defects the browser leg caught that no jsdom suite could, and this is the case for the leg existing.** (1) Dropping the click gesture changed nothing a user sees, because clicking a `<button>` **focuses** it and focus-open then showed the bubble instantly — and a touch **tap** synthesises the compatibility mouse sequence with nothing following to move the pointer away, so the emulated hover stuck and the bubble appeared 500 ms after every tap. Both are suppressed by reading the pointer (`onPointerDown`, `event.pointerType`). Neither could have been *found* in jsdom — nothing there focuses on click, and a tap has no compatibility mouse sequence — but both are pinned there now; `pointerType` needs a hand-built event to survive, because jsdom implements no `PointerEvent` and RTL's `fireEvent.pointerOver(el, {pointerType})` therefore delivers `null` (measured, and the reason a first attempt read as unassertable). (2) With the component correct the bubble still landed **559 px** from its trigger: `.tooltip-host` is `inline-flex`, but a flex item is blockified to `flex` and then stretched by `align-items: stretch`, so inside `div.flex--col` the host came out 1222 px wide around a 35 px button. Fixed with `width: fit-content` (a definite cross size, so `stretch` stops applying); `width` only, because `height: fit-content` would also stop the host stretching vertically in a row container, which is how a wrapped button gets its height today. **Every jsdom suite was green while the shipped tooltip was unusable.**

  **Finding 4 closed, and half of it was never broken.** `tooltip.less`'s four corner placements did put the bubble on its own trigger — `.tooltip.left`/`.right` set `top: 50%` at the same specificity as `.top`/`.bottom`, so a corner class string matched both and the axis came out over-constrained. Fixed by writing the losing offset back per corner rather than by raising specificity. A **second, independent** defect had to go with it: the verdict required every corner to align to its host's *left* edge, which `top right` and `bottom right` cannot do by definition, so those two would have stayed red after the CSS was right. All four were genuinely broken in CSS — `PLACEMENTS_OVERLAPPING_HOST` recorded both top corners sitting on their own host — and an earlier draft of this section wrongly said the right corners had been placing correctly all along. 8 of 8 now. The component asks for `top`, not the wrapper's `top left` — that was SUIR's own default rather than a meta's request, so the bubble is centred above its trigger instead of left-aligned above it.

  **The accessibility trade, stated rather than sold.** 1.4.13 wants hovered content hoverable; this bubble keeps `pointer-events: none` because without it the bubble sits under the pointer, `mouseleave` fires on the host and the tooltip flickers — so the pointer over the bubble is really over what is behind it and it closes, exactly as the wrapper behaved without `hoverable`. Hoverable text and a non-interactive bubble are mutually exclusive and the bubble stays non-interactive. And a **touch-only device now has no way to see a tooltip at all**: the same trade desktop makes (the tooltip is supplementary, the node's action is not), and the reason the content must never be the only place information lives. `pointer-events: none` also invalidated an *instrument* — `document.elementFromPoint` skips such elements, so every "is the bubble painted here" assertion silently inverted; `e2e/fixtures.js` grew `paintedTopmostAt`, which re-enables pointer events for the probe alone.

  **An adversarial review after the step was green found three real bugs, and that is the lesson worth carrying to step 3.** Six independent lenses over the change set, each finding then put to three skeptics told to refute it: 55 candidates, 13 upheld, 8 refuted — and 34 left with **no verdict at all** because the verifying agents hit a session limit, which is recorded here because "not verified" is not "refuted" and the difference is the whole value of the panel. The three code defects, none of which any gate could see:
  - **`aria-describedby` was ASSIGNED, not appended.** A trigger can already point at something of its own — a form control at its validation message is the case in this product — so the error text was unlinked for exactly as long as the tooltip was open: present while a sighted user reads the tooltip, absent from the announcement that matters. Now appended, trigger's own ids first, because order is announcement order.
  - **The pointer guard outlived its interaction.** `fromPointer` was cleared only on blur, so a `pointerdown` that moved no focus left it standing for the component's life and suppressed the next KEYBOARD focus — the guard protecting the click removal was disabling the keyboard path the same removal created. Now one-shot, and cleared when the pointer leaves.
  - **A controlled `open={true}` never reported its first close.** `reported` starts `false` (right for an uncontrolled tooltip, which starts closed), so a tooltip mounted open took the early return in `change()` and a host trusting `onClose` stayed open for good. `reported` now tracks the effective state.

  Each fix is pinned by a test **verified to fail when the fix is reverted** — checked one at a time, because a regression test nobody has seen fail is a guess. The review also caught eight prose defects in this very change set, including two claims of my own that measurement refuted: that the right corner placements "were placing correctly all along" (they were not — both top corners sat on their own host, and the assertion defect was a *second*, independent problem) and that "no class can defeat" the hover reveal at equal specificity (`&.show { animation-delay: 0s }` four lines later does exactly that; the real reason mount-on-open is necessary is that the reveal has no delay to inherit).

  **The re-run of the unverified findings was the most productive pass of the three, and it found six tests that were passing for the wrong reason — four of them broken BY the first fix pass.** The panel's own bookkeeping was fixed first: a finding whose three skeptics all died had been filed as "refuted" with no reason attached, so 34 results were indistinguishable from judgements. Re-run with `unverified` as its own outcome: **27 upheld, 17 refuted, 11 still unverified** of 55. **SUPERSEDED BY A THIRD PASS, recorded in Appendix C: every candidate now has a verdict — 28 upheld, 27 refuted, 0 unverified of 55, with all 171 agents completing (28+27=55, so the ledger balances). No unverified bucket survives; the second-pass figures are kept because they are what this narrative reasoned from. Caveat on the strength of that: the only in-repo record of the counts is Appendix C itself — there is no per-finding ledger artifact under `docs/` or `.claude/` — so "no work remains" is well supported while the exact 28/27 split is not independently re-derivable.** The refuted bucket is mostly the first pass's own fixes being confirmed as fixed.
  - **Two more real behaviour bugs.** A pointer that merely crossed the trigger closed a bubble the KEYBOARD had opened, with focus still on it and nothing to reopen it but Tab out and back. And the pointer guard was defeated by the event order of an ordinary click that moves focus *within* the host — `pointerdown` → `focusout` → `focusin`, and clearing the flag on blur wiped it before the incoming focus could consume it, so a mouse click read as keyboard-caused and opened the bubble instantly. Reachable from meta, because `Render.js` wraps any node carrying a `tooltip`, containers included.
  - **Four edge cases, each a wrong answer to a caller.** `disabled` arriving while an open was armed reported `onOpen` for a bubble that never appeared — and the fix had to read `disabled` from a ref, because a pending open is a closure from the render that scheduled it. A caller-controlled `open` was advancing internal state underneath, so dropping the prop made the tooltip jump to a state nobody asked for. A function body was invoked on every render of a CLOSED tooltip — a call into caller code, on every page load. An empty body still mounted a bubble with `role="tooltip"` and an `aria-describedby` pointing at nothing.
  - **Six tests that gated nothing.** The only browser gate for Escape closed the bubble by blur before the key was pressed (the setup I had written to avoid the click gesture). "A click outside closes it" was satisfied by Playwright moving the pointer off the trigger first. The clipping test measured a static harness control instead of the bubble it had opened, because `anyBubble` matches any `span.tooltip` and the section renders one from page load. Two scroll assertions still counted the semantic-ui-react portal selector — vacuously true — which is the exact defect class this step claims to have swept. And nine assertions compared a reference constant with a hard-coded copy of its own value: they can only fail if someone edits `reference.js`.
  - **One claim of mine was inverted, not merely imprecise:** `pointer-events: none` does not prevent a flicker. The bubble is a CHILD of the host that owns the mouse handlers, and `mouseleave` does not fire when the pointer moves onto a descendant — so removing the declaration would make the bubble *hoverable* while the trigger became unusable. The measured reason it exists is that the bubble is painted over its own trigger and would otherwise swallow the pointer. Corrected in six places.
  - **And the prop arithmetic is now derived rather than estimated:** `Popup.handledProps` (29) ∪ `Portal.handledProps` (22) = 45, splitting 9 accepted / 19 warned / 17 silent. `mouseEnterDelay` was moved from silent to warned — it was Semantic's name for `delay`, the one silent drop a real meta could plausibly contain. The development warning itself reaches nobody outside this repo, because the library builds in production mode only; that is now stated where it was previously offered as the migration path.

  **Gates.** jsdom: 160 suites / 2477 tests / 38 snapshots. Chrome: 39/39 across four specs and two projects. `docs:props:check` green with `TooltipPop` moved from `WRAPPERS` to `IN_HOUSE` in the generator — which required making `subcomponents` and the shared `Part` reader optional, since the in-house shape had assumed a component family (`Table`) and this one is a single element. One `-u` on the 38-example baseline: the `.tooltip-host` wrapper is new markup at tooltip sites, 10 lines of snapshot diff.

- **Step 3 — `Dropdown` (L, ~2–4 weeks incl. regression) — SHIPPED (parts 1 and 2; `63f02316`, `bddf7935`).** The plan's largest remaining F1 step is complete, and the part-by-part write-up further down this section records it: `Dropdown.js:4` is `import DropDown from './Listbox'` — the SUIR element is gone from the leaf — and `src/core/components/Listbox.js` (357 lines, `role="listbox"` at `:282`) is the replacement, with zero new dependencies. The scoping that follows is the step as it was PLANNED, kept for the reasoning it carries. Step 0 confirmed the L and relocated it: not `search`/`multiple`/`allowAdditions`/`clearable` (unused everywhere), but the keyboard/a11y matrix, the cascading flows, `upward`'s auto-flip (an autoControlled prop — left unset SUIR measures viewport space and flips itself, so the replacement owes the behaviour as well as the prop) and the `.ui.selection.dropdown` CSS contract, which couples this step to Step 4 more tightly than the sequencing implies. **BOTH RESOLVED, and neither is an ordering hazard any more (re-checked 2026-09-17).** The CSS coupling is retired because step 4 shipped: `.ui.selection.dropdown` still reaches `static/all.css`, but now out of `src/style/vendor/semantic-dropdown.less` — code this repo owns and regenerates — imported by `_semantic.less` as `@import "../vendor/semantic-dropdown"`, with `semantic-ui-less` gone from `package.json` and `package-lock.json`. `upward` was settled the other way round: the PROP survives (destructured at `Listbox.js:144`, emitted as a class modifier at `:293`, declared `PropTypes.bool` at `:350`, and listed as a consumer-only `Select` attribute at `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md:294`), while the AUTO-FLIP was deliberately NOT reimplemented, on part 1's measurement that it never fired — there is no measurement or flip logic anywhere in `Listbox.js`. That resolves the contradiction between this sentence and the part-1 measurement recorded below. Two viable paths:
  - **(a) Headless engine + own markup — recommended:** `downshift` (`useSelect`/`useCombobox`/`useMultipleSelection`) provides WAI-ARIA combobox behavior and keyboard navigation; we render semantic-compatible markup (`ui selection dropdown`, `ui label` chips) for CSS continuity. Mature, small, unstyled — consistent with the "own components, scoped CSS" principle.
  - (b) Fully hand-rolled — full control, but the combobox keyboard/a11y matrix is precisely where hand-rolled implementations leak; choose only if zero-dependency is mandatory.
  The wrapper's own logic (sanitization, dedup, additions handling, cascading reset, `onChange` signature) is **kept as-is** — only the `<DropDown …/>` element at the bottom is replaced. **HONOURED EXACTLY (verified 2026-09-17):** only the import at `Dropdown.js:4` changed, from `semantic-ui-react` to `./Listbox`; option sanitisation (`:178–181`), the cascading-reset effect (`:184–190`, calling `onChange(validOptions[0], props.name)`), the parent-value sync (`:157–163`), the readonly→disabled mapping (`:192`) and the selection default (`:193`) all stayed put. (Additions handling is the one exception, and deliberately so: `allowAdditions` was REMOVED at part 2 — see below.) One extra contract discovered by the re-audit: the form adapter special-cases `InputComponent.displayName === 'Dropdown'` (`form/utils.js:193,199`) — the replacement must keep `displayName = 'Dropdown'`, or that adapter branch is refactored in the same PR. **KEPT, and the adapter was never refactored (verified 2026-09-17):** `Dropdown.js:317` sets `displayName` on the named function export at `:111`, with `export default React.memo(Dropdown)` at `:335`; the two reads are unchanged and sit at `form/utils.js:192,198` — one line up from the `:193,199` recorded here, which is trivial drift rather than a moved contract. **Step 0 sharpened it:** the branch works only because `DropdownField.js` imports the *named* export, since `React.memo(...)` does not carry `displayName` (the default export has none). The named-vs-default split is therefore part of the contract, not an implementation detail, and `scripts/__tests__/wrapper-prop-reference.contract.test.js` now pins both facts. Gate: the behavioral layer of the §9.5 suite + the mandatory visual/keyboard/a11y suite + full example QA (full-DOM snapshots are expected to change here — regenerate deliberately); the cascading-Select flows driven from `rules.js`/`mapper.js` are the regression hotspot.

  **Part 1 — SHIPPED (measurement + gate, deliberately a separate PR from the swap).** The step-2 lesson applied: measure and gate first, because there the measurement changed what the swap was for. The gate problem is the same one and it is still here — `Dropdown.behavior.test.js` (21 tests) opens with `jest.mock('semantic-ui-react')` and asserts the props object handed to SUIR, so it passes against a component that renders nothing; `Dropdown.test.js` (26) and `Dropdown.more.test.js` (16) render real SUIR but reach it through class names the rewrite is free to drop.

  **SIX DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED, each pinned by a test verified to fail when the fix is reverted.** Five are in wrapper code the swap KEEPS, so they would have been ported faithfully:
  - **`getFormsData()` threw for the simplest possible Select.** `changeOptionOrderForSelectFields` destructured `mapOptions` above the branches that test its type, so a `view: 'Select'` node with a string value and no `mapOptions` raised `Cannot destructure property 'text' of 'mapOptions' as it is undefined` — **in the submit path**. Every tracked example escaped it only by declaring `mapOptions` in its JSON, which is why the whole corpus was green over it.
  - **The wrapper threw on absent or null `options`.** `switch (typeof options[0])` — reachable from a bare `{view: 'Dropdown', name: 'x'}`, and from a `name`-bound path that resolves to null. Only `options: []` ever rendered, and the comment beside it claimed the opposite. Absent and null are now the empty state.
  - **The cascading reset lied to the host twice.** It called `onChange(String(value))` with arity 1, so a host whose options carry numbers was told to select `'7'` where the option is `7` — a `===` match missed it — and the wrapper's own documented signature is `(value, name, event)`. Both fixed; there is no event for a programmatic reset, so the third argument is genuinely absent rather than faked.
  - **A `multiple` dropdown mounted with `value={[]}` reported a selection the user never made.** The reset guard tested `== null || === ''`, and `String([])` is `''`, which is in no option list — so `onChange(firstOption)` fired on mount.
  - **`optionsLabel` duplicated on every addition**, because `onAddItem` wrote the label-appended array back into state.
  - **The caller's `id` landed on two elements** (the help block and, through the rest bag, Semantic's listbox) and the error/info text was never announced. The help block now carries `${id}-help` and the control points at it with `aria-describedby` — the first addition to this component's forwarded surface, which is why the audited-surface counter went UP.

  **Measured in real Chrome, and it changes what the swap owes.** `upward`'s auto-flip — which this plan called an obligation the replacement inherits — **did not fire at any tested condition**, including with the menu overflowing the viewport by 8 px; the corpus dropdown has two options, so a bigger overflow is not reachable by shrinking the window, and whether it can fire at all here is NOT established. **The in-house control flips since 2026-10-05**, when `upward` is unset: measured in Chromium, a select at the bottom edge of an 800 px viewport opened its list at 803–968 px before, out of sight, and at 598–763 px, upward, after. One at the top still opens downward. Also measured: Tab reaches the listbox **and opens it**; `Enter` toggles rather than commits; arrows commit as they move (`selectOnNavigation`) and Escape does not restore; **`Home`, `End`, PageUp/PageDown and typeahead do nothing at all** — `grep` over the whole SUIR Dropdown module never reads those keys. **That is a measurement of the REMOVED implementation, and part 2 superseded it rather than leaving a parity gap: the replacement writes every one of them.** `Listbox.js:77` handles `Home`, `:78` `End`, `:60–61` `PageDown: 5` / `PageUp: -5`, and `:112` `typeaheadFor()` with a 700 ms reset at `:65`. `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md:212` and `:347` record the change: these keys were measured ABSENT from the library, so there was nothing to preserve and everything to write. The current behaviour is a strict improvement over this baseline.

  **On the audit method, since it is now three for three:** seven measurement agents produced 99 load-bearing claims and independent re-derivation **corrected 59 of them** while reproducing 40. Two of the corrections mattered here — the submit crash was attributed to the wrong file, and the sanitiser was said to have no numeric branch when it has one that simply does not translate. Nothing was encoded from an unverified claim. The agents were also allowed to write throwaway test files into the repo and left ~50 behind in 17 `probe-*` directories, which inflated a full-suite run before it was noticed: **do not run the full suite against a tree that agents are still writing to.**

  **THE PRIMITIVE — DECIDED: hand-rolled, no new dependency.** The plan recommended `downshift` and explicitly said to choose the zero-dependency path "only if zero-dependency is mandatory"; the maintainers chose it, and the reason is the one the recommendation did not weigh: this step exists inside a workstream whose NEXT step (3½) removes a dependency in order to stop capping the host's React, and "we removed `semantic-ui-react` and added `downshift`" is a poor answer to that. **(3½ HAS SINCE SHIPPED — `09719a91` "F1 step 3½: remove semantic-ui-react from dependencies (closes Phase 5a)": verified 2026-09-17, `semantic-ui-react` appears 0 times in `package.json` — the only mentions left are the `no-restricted-imports` guard — 0 times in `package-lock.json`, and `node_modules/semantic-ui-react` does not exist. The forward reference is discharged. Our OWN `peerDependencies` still cap at `^18`, which is deliberate, and `react-final-form` / `react-final-form-arrays` still carry ≤`^18` peers of their own.)**

  What was measured before deciding, so the trade is on record rather than assumed. `downshift@9.4.0` would NOT have re-created the problem 3½ solves — its peer is `react: '>=16.12.0'`, an open upper bound, so React 19 stays unblocked — and it is tree-shakeable (`sideEffects: false`, an ESM build in its `exports` map), so the 53.9 KB of its minified UMD is not what we would have paid for `useSelect` alone. It would have brought five transitive dependencies (`@babel/runtime`, `compute-scroll-into-view`, `prop-types`, `react-is`, `tslib`), two of which are already present through `semantic-ui-react`. So the library option was viable on every technical count; it was rejected on the dependency-direction argument, not on a defect.

  **THE API NARROWED FIRST, on the maintainers' decision: `search`, `multiple` and `allowAdditions` are REMOVED.** They are what made a hand-rolled replacement expensive — filtering, diacritics-insensitive matching, a search input, multi-value handling, a free-text addition item with case-insensitive dedup — and nothing declares them. The evidence, checked on every channel this repo tracks rather than assumed: no tracked example meta declares any of the three; `CONSUMER_ONLY_ATTRIBUTES`, which exists precisely to record what consumer metas use and the examples do not, lists `disabled`/`upward`/`validate` for `Select` and NOTHING for `Dropdown`; and the single `"multiple": false` in the corpus is on a file-upload input, not a select. The changelog had also invited consumers to report using exactly these props, and no report came — that entry is now closed in the changelog rather than left hanging.

  Three more names went with them because they could not outlive them: `onSearch` and `onAddItem` are their callbacks, and `autofocus` only ever became `searchInput={{autoFocus: true}}`, which does nothing on a control with no search input — removing search left it dead rather than merely unused. `clearable` is untouched: the wrapper never read it, so it rides to the library and goes with the swap instead. **It did go with the swap, and is now explicitly handled rather than silent (verified 2026-09-17):** `Dropdown.js:52` lists it among the stripped-and-warned names — `:32` records why, since it produced React's "Received `true` for a non-boolean attribute" warning once `Listbox`'s open rest spread would have made it an attribute — and `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md:262` carries its `Dropped`-table entry.

  **This is a BREAKING change and is recorded as one** — in `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md`, which gained a `Dropped` table for wrapped components (it had one only for in-house ones, and prose in a summary is not where anyone looks for a prop), and in the changelog with what a consumer loses silently if their meta declares one. Step 5 records the semver call for the whole exit; on this evidence this part of it is a **major**. **THE CALL IS NOW RECORDED (2026-09-11): the owners chose a MINOR for the whole exit** — `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md:265`, which deliberately leaves this major-on-the-evidence argument standing rather than rewriting it, "because the evidence and the decision are different things and a reader is entitled to both". The tension between the two is therefore recorded, not hidden, and it remains a live judgement to re-confirm at publish time, since the release itself is still deferred.

  **What it bought, measured.** The wrapper went 330 → 271 lines. The audited surface shrank with it: the wrapper's own intercepted props 23 → 20, and the tier-2 forwarded set — "published but nothing uses it" — 13 → 7, because six of those thirteen were the free-text and multi-select machinery. Eleven tests went with their subject and three were re-aimed at what survives; two of the three had been passing VACUOUSLY already, exercising a removed prop while asserting nothing about it.

  **And one part-1 fix became unnecessary**, which is worth recording rather than quietly deleting: `onAddItem` used to write the option list back into state including the appended `optionsLabel`, so every addition appended another label. With additions gone nothing writes options back, so the bug is unreachable and the fix is removed with it. A second removal came from measuring rather than reasoning: the case-insensitive value collapse in `onChange` looked like it survived independently, but it only ever fired for a value the user TYPED — a value picked from a list is an option's own value, so nothing could differ in case. Keeping it would have been dead code behind a comment claiming otherwise.

  **A third option the plan never listed, and why it is not taken: a native `<select>`.** It is the
  true zero-dependency answer and it would give the whole keyboard matrix for free — arrows, Home/End,
  typeahead and the platform's own accessibility, none of it written or maintained here. There is
  even ~~an unused `components/Select.js` in the pack that renders one.~~ **CORRECTED
  2026-09-17: `components/Select.js` is NOT unused, and the clause is struck rather than
  scheduled — acting on it would be a regression.** `InputNative.js:4` imports it and `:121`
  returns `<Select {...props} />` for `type: 'select'`; that runs through `Input.js:6` to
  `modules/form/inputs/InputField.js:2` and `pages/main/components/LocalDraftTableRow.js:3`, a
  live production chain, and it has its own `components/__tests__/Select.test.js`. Deleting it
  would break every `type: 'select'` field. Nothing has acted on the line — `Select.js` is
  intact on `master` at 85 lines — so this is a documentation fix, not a release blocker. The
  rest of the paragraph stands: a native `<select>` is still rejected, and for the CSS reason
  given below. It is rejected on CSS, not on
  behaviour: every loaded dropdown rule is keyed on `.ui.selection.dropdown` with a `.menu` of
  `.item` children, and a native option list is not stylable — so taking it would drag step 4's CSS
  re-homing into step 3 and change how every select in the product looks. Recorded because
  "hand-rolled" now means re-implementing what a browser gives away, and that cost should be a
  known choice rather than an oversight.

  **What that makes the swap owe.** The plan's warning stands and is now the main risk to manage: the combobox keyboard/a11y matrix is "precisely where hand-rolled implementations leak". Part 1's measurements cut the work in two directions — LESS behaviour to port than assumed (`upward`'s auto-flip never fires, `Home`/`End`/`PageUp`/`PageDown` and typeahead do nothing today, so none of it can be regressed) but the matrix has to be BUILT rather than carried over, and it is the half no existing test covers. The gate part 1 shipped is what makes that tractable: 9 behaviour tests that all fail against a control rendering nothing, the class-token CSS contract, and the browser leg.

  **Part 1 — SHIPPED (measurement + gate; product code touched only to fix what the measurement found).** The gate now exists, and it was accepted by the only test that means anything for a gate: **every one of `Dropdown.gate.test.js`'s 9 tests fails when the inner control is stubbed to render nothing**, checked before the file was committed. Before it, 22 tests passed against exactly that.

  What the gate covers, all through handles a replacement cannot avoid (roles, displayed text, callbacks, DOM attributes) rather than through `.ui.dropdown` class names: one listbox rendered closed showing the first option; options mounted only on open, with `aria-expanded` tracking it; a click committing the option and reporting `(value, name, event)`; engine props, `name` and `label` absent from the DOM while the name is still reported; ~~free-text addition, prepended and announced; search filtering;~~ **(struck 2026-09-17: part 2 REMOVED `allowAdditions` and `search` — recorded above — so the gate covers neither. `grep -i` for `addition` or `search` over `Dropdown.gate.test.js` returns nothing, and the file is **7** tests today, not the 9 recorded just above, because these two went with the props.)** `readonly` as a control that cannot be reached or opened; the cascading instruction to the host; and the a11y defect inventory.

  **`css.dropdown-contract.test.js` — the class-string ↔ CSS join the tooltip had and the dropdown did not**, even though `modules/dropdown` is the largest semantic module in the compiled output. It does not pin a selector list (over fifty rules reach five nodes; nobody reads that, and it says nothing about which parts matter). It pins **what each class token is worth**: the token is removed from the rendered node and the scoped rules that stop matching are counted. Measured: `ui` and `dropdown` are worth all 13 rules on the control and `selection` 12, so `mapper.js` defaulting `selection` is load-bearing rather than cosmetic; the icon needs BOTH `icon` and `dropdown` on the same element (13 each); `menu` carries 11 of the menu's 14 with `transition` worth 4 and `visible` 3; `item` 6 of an option's 7. Accepted the same way: dropping `selection` fails 3 of its 4 tests. Class ORDER is deliberately not pinned — it cannot change what a selector matches. **Re-measured 2026-10-06:** jsdom's selector engine, nwsapi, answered `:focus-within` false up to 2.2.23 even with focus inside; 2.2.28 matches it, so the counts now include the four `:focus-within` focus repeats Chromium always applied to the open, focused dropdown: `ui` and `dropdown` 15, `selection` 14, the icon's two tokens 14 each, `menu` 12. The test's own comment names the rules.

  **Two tests that could never fail, revived** — one had no `expect` at all (its comment said so), the other looked for `.icon.pointer` where the rendered class is `icon-dropdown pointer` and guarded its only assertion with `if (icon)`. Both now measure the behaviour both ways and both were verified to fail when it breaks.

  **The `[R->I]` a11y facts were mis-tagged as browser-only.** `role="alert"` announcing the selection, and the five missing combobox attributes, are attribute facts — exactly what jsdom does have. Both are pinned in the gate now, so a regression fails on every commit rather than only in the `browser` job; the browser leg keeps them because only it can say whether the accessibility TREE agrees with the attributes.

  **The action-argument collision — FIXED, after first being recorded as too risky to fix.** `getFunctionFromString` composes `'setState,categoryX'` as `(...caller) => setStates(...caller, 'categoryX')`: the caller's arguments first, the meta's appended. That is unambiguous only for a caller of FIXED arity, and the dropdown's is three — `onChange(value, name, event)` — so `setStates(value, keyPath)` read the field's `name` as the state path while the configured path arrived fourth and was ignored. Every `view: 'Select'` whose two differ wrote to the wrong place; `mapper.js` worked around it for stable-value Selects only, with a comment saying exactly this.

  The first pass recorded it as pinned-not-fixed on the grounds that every candidate fix reached beyond the dropdown. **Two of those three grounds did not survive being checked.** The tracked corpus configures action arguments in exactly ONE place, and only two actions take them at all (`setState`, arity 2, and one `reset,0`, arity 0) — so the surface is small and knowable, not sprawling. What DID survive is the argument against fixing it in the composer: a positional convention there applies to every action, and none suits all of them — `setStates` wants the configured value as its PATH while `popupAlert(title, content)` configured as `'popup,SomeTitle'` plainly wants it FIRST, as the title.

  So the fix is local to `setStates`, which now resolves the path as **the last STRING argument**. Measured against all four real call shapes, that is the only rule correct in all of them: "second argument" is wrong for the dropdown (the defect), and "last argument" is wrong when nothing is configured and the caller's last argument is the DOM event. The degenerate shape — nothing configured, one argument — stays the silent no-op it was, because `set(state, undefined, value)` returns the state unchanged and turning that into an error is a separate decision. `rules.set-state-path.test.js` measures each shape and fails on the dropdown row when the resolution is reverted; `transforms.action-args.test.js` keeps the composer's half, which is unchanged and correct.

  **Gates.** jsdom 164 suites / 2504 tests / 38 snapshots on React 16.14, 17 and 18.3; Chrome 40/40; coverage 100/100/100/100 on `Dropdown.js` and `transforms.js`; `lint:js`, `lint:css`, `docs:props:check` green.

  **Part 2 — SHIPPED (the swap).** `src/core/components/Listbox.js`, 340 lines, `role="listbox"`, no dependency. `Dropdown.js` changed by one import line and kept everything else it owns: the `onChange(value, name, event)` signature, option sanitisation, the cascading reset, the help-block wiring. `semantic-ui-react` is now referenced by **no file in `src` by any mechanism** — import, `import()`, `require` or `jest.mock` — which is what step 3½ was waiting for.

  **The keyboard matrix, built rather than ported, and it is a deliberate behaviour change.** Arrows move a CURSOR conveyed by `aria-activedescendant`, Enter commits, Escape closes having reported nothing. The library committed as the arrows moved (`selectOnNavigation`) and on blur (`selectOnBlur`), so a user who arrowed past an option had already changed the form with no keyboard way back. `UIRender.listbox-behavior.test.js` pinned that as it WAS, with a comment saying that if the replacement adopted the pattern where Escape reverts, "this test is the one that has to be rewritten, and the rewrite is the visible record of the behaviour change" — so the rewrite is there, naming what it replaced. Added, none of which existed before: `Home`/`End`, PageUp/PageDown, and typeahead with a 700 ms window where a REPEATED character cycles through the options starting with it. Kept as measured: wrapping at both ends, ArrowDown opening a closed control and advancing in the same press, focus NOT opening the list.

  **`role="alert"` is gone from the corpus, and the count is the proof it was one thing.** Semantic rendered the selected-value text with `role="alert" aria-live`, announcing the current value as an interruption. §9.5 recorded that every `alert` in the role census was one dropdown and that all of them should go to zero at this step: 12 entries across 11 examples dropped to zero, and `presentation: N` took their place for the same N — the `.menu` between the listbox and its options, which needs a role of its own or the owned-element relationship breaks. Neither role is filtered out of the census; both are still scanned, so a re-introduced live region shows up as a new entry.

  **Three defects the gates found in the new code, each now pinned by a test verified to fail when the fix is reverted:**
  - **`aria-disabled` was missing.** A `role="listbox"` div cannot carry the native attribute, so being unavailable has to be *said* — and `readonly` fields in the corpus rely on it. Caught by `UIRender.listbox-behavior.test.js`, which had pinned the library's behaviour.
  - **Props Semantic consumed became DOM attributes.** SUIR declared `search`/`multiple`/`allowAdditions`/`clearable`/`required` as handled props, so passing one used to be harmless; `Listbox` spreads what it does not destructure onto its element. Measured: `required` rendered as `required=""` on a div, `clearable` produced React's "Received `true` for a non-boolean attribute" warning. `Dropdown.js` now strips them and warns once per name in development — the mechanism `TooltipPop` already had — and `required` is destructured instead, because the wrapper reads it for its own class.
  - **Option ids were not deterministic across renders.** `aria-activedescendant` needs an id, the prefix is a per-mount counter, and `mapper.js` passes `lazyLoad={false}` for `view: "Dropdown"` — so the ids sat in the CLOSED DOM. The 38-example baseline renders every example twice and compares, which is exactly what caught it. A closed control emits no option id at all now; a cursor only exists while the list is open.

  **The DOM baseline changed in 11 of 38 examples, and every line of it was accounted for before regenerating** — the account is in the suite's own header so the next reader does not have to re-derive it. Beyond the `alert` node: the option text is no longer wrapped in `<span class="text">` (checked against the compiled CSS, not assumed — the only loaded rules reaching a `.text` inside an item require an icon inside the option, which nothing renders); `aria-checked` is gone as redundant with `aria-selected`; the inline `style="pointer-events: all"` is gone because the rule it defended against is unreachable here; `role="presentation"` is added; and class token ORDER moved, which cannot change what a selector matches.

  **Two dead paths deleted after reading the only caller, not after assuming.** `typeaheadFor` fell back to `option.value` when `text` was absent — impossible from `Dropdown.js`, which declares `text` required and whose every sanitiser branch emits one, and inconsistent with the trigger, which renders `text` alone. And three `cursor === -1 ? selectedIndex : cursor` expressions could never substitute anything, because both open paths seed the cursor from the selection. `Listbox.js` is not exported from the library, so its one caller is the whole world — which is what makes that argument sound rather than convenient.

  **What the generated prop page became, since a component that imports nothing cannot stay in a list whose entries are asserted to import SUIR.** This is the cost step 1 surfaced and step 3 paid in full: `WRAPPERS`, `WRAPPER_CURATION` and `FORWARDED_CURATION` are all empty now, and the tier vocabulary went with them — tiers ranked a PARITY CHECKLIST, and there is no second implementation to reach parity with. The 25 forwarded `Dropdown` rows were re-homed individually (consumed props to `props`, removed features to `dropped` alongside Semantic's own names for the machinery behind them, plain passthroughs to a `passthrough` note), and `FORWARDED_CURATION`'s header records where each went. The scan that enforced "only inside the components pack" now enforces the stronger "nowhere at all", and it no longer throws on finding zero: that throw existed because "nothing found" and "the regexes rotted" are indistinguishable from inside the scan, so the proof moved to the contract test, which runs all four patterns against a synthetic source. Three renderer defects were fixed on the way, two of them visible on the already-shipped page: an empty table header printed for a component with no subcomponents, a hardcoded "All six share one implementation" under components that have none, and `Table`'s reasoning about reachable spreads printed under all three (it is derived per component now).

  **Gates.** jsdom 166 suites / 2538 tests / 38 snapshots on React 16.14, 17 and 18.3; Chrome 41/41 including a new browser test that the open listbox names its cursor with `aria-activedescendant` and that the cursor moves; coverage 100/100/100/100 on `Dropdown.js` (branches 99 → 100, the uncovered ones belonged to the removed features) and a new floor at the same numbers for `Listbox.js`; `lint:js`, `lint:css`, `docs:props:check`, `docs:views:check` green. One caveat recorded rather than hidden, and my first explanation of it was wrong: **three suite-level failures across this work and step 3½, none reproducible** — `examples.behavior-contract.test.js` on React 18, `asField.test.js` on the React 17 leg, and `dataKindPush.edge-cases.test.js` on React 18. Each was green immediately afterwards, and there have since been eight consecutive clean full runs including two on a cleared jest cache. What is KNOWN is the shape: zero failing tests reported and a test count short by exactly that file's test count, which means the suite never ran rather than an assertion breaking. I wrote here that it was contention, because the first two happened while another run or the Playwright web server was live; the third did not, so that explanation does not hold and is withdrawn rather than quietly kept. Two further hypotheses were tested and refuted: `src/style/__tests__/setup.js`'s `node_modules` write (it is already content-guarded and uses a pid-unique temp file, so no worker writes after the first) and a cold transform cache. **What is missing is the message** — all three were found through a filtered grep of the run output, so the actual failure text was never captured. Next occurrence: re-run with the full output kept (`npx jest > run.log 2>&1`) before anything else. Recorded at this length because an intermittent suite that never runs is worth a real diagnosis, and guessing at it twice already produced two wrong answers. **Diagnosed 2026-09-30:** a jest worker killed by SIGSEGV, V8's garbage-collector bug nodejs/node#62393 on Node 24, measured in 3 of 80 full runs on macOS arm64 and never on CI's Linux. The fix is backported to Node's 24.x staging branch; until a release carries it, such a run is rerun and both runs are reported. Separately, `asField.test.js` prints a pre-existing `Invalid DOM property \`readonly\`` warning from its own test fixture on every React version — unrelated, and not introduced here.

- **Step 3½ — exit the dependency (closes 5a) — SHIPPED.** With steps 1–3 landed the bundle no longer referenced SUIR, so `semantic-ui-react` is out of `dependencies`. This, not the code swap, is what unblocks React 19 for npm hosts: while the entry remained, SUIR's own `react ^16.8 || ^17 || ^18` peers capped the host's React (§2.6-11). `semantic-ui-less` is a devDependency and stays until Step 4.

  **What it actually took, beyond deleting a line.** The `no-restricted-imports` guard lost its `excludedFiles` exemption: `src/core/components` was the one place allowed to import the package, and with the package gone there is no exemption to grant, so the rule now covers all of `src` and answers with a message rather than a module-not-found. `node_modules` needed `npm prune` as well as `npm install` — the package stays on disk otherwise, and a local run that can still resolve it is a local run that does not match CI. And the React-19 job's `--legacy-peer-deps` comment went from listing two caps to one. **Inventory corrected 2026-09-17 (re-measured):** one is the wrong count — **two** direct dependencies still declare `"react": "^16.8.0 || ^17.0.0 || ^18.0.0"`, `react-final-form` 6.5.9 **and** `react-final-form-arrays` 3.1.4, so the flag is still overriding two peer caps, not one. Measured by reading `peerDependencies.react` in every installed `package.json`: those two are the only entries capped below 19 — the `rc-*` tree (`rc-picker` 4.11.3 and its `@rc-component` / `rc-util` transitives) declares an open-ended `>=16.9.0`, which caps nothing. The flag's behaviour is unchanged; only the inventory was false. The claim as it stood: "`react-final-form` 6.5.9 is the only package left declaring a `react ≤^18` peer (§9.7-F4)". The same single-entry list is written into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` above the `Install React 19 over the lock` step and needs the same correction. **Moot since 2026-09-30:** the step and its flag went with the advisory job; the `react-19` leg installs React 19 from a fixture under plain `npm ci`.

  **What it deliberately did NOT do: widen our own `peerDependencies`.** They still read `^16.14.0 || ^17.0.0 || ^18.0.0`. Removing the cap that came from a *dependency* is this step; declaring that we *support* React 19 is a claim about our own code, and the `react-19-advisory` job is still advisory (`continue-on-error`). It is green — and has been since the swap, which is the first tangible evidence the exit works — but making it a gate and widening the range belongs to the phase that owns React 19, not here. The blocker is gone; the claim is not yet made. **Made 2026-09-30 (§8):** the peer range includes `^19.0.0`, and `react-19` gates.

- **Step 4 — CSS exit (M, ~1 week).** Re-home the 5 semantic-ui-less modules as in-house LESS under `src/style` (starting from the *compiled output* of the current build guarantees pixel parity; prefixwrap scoping under `.ui-render` already applies). Delete `semantic-ui-less`, the `theme.config` webpack aliases (`webpack.demo.config.mjs:89`, `webpack.library.config.mjs:69`), `_semantic.less`, and the dead `.variables`/`.overrides` files. **CORRECTION (2026-09-11, measured): the `javascriptEnabled` requirement is NOT semantic's and this step will not clear it.** Rendering `src/style/index.less` with `javascriptEnabled: false` fails at `src/style/_variables.less:23` — on our own `` `Math.random()` ``, a cache-busting `@version`. Removing `semantic-ui-less` changes nothing about that; unpinning the flag means removing that one line first. The earlier text here claimed the opposite and was never tested. Two obligations ride this step: copying compiled semantic-ui-less CSS requires carrying its **MIT license notice** (attribution header / THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES entry), and the §9.9-H8 decision on the unscoped `html`/`body`/`*` reset (§2.6-7) is implemented in the owned CSS here.

  **FIRST HALF — SHIPPED (2026-09-11): three modules deleted outright, no re-homing.** `modules/popup`, `elements/label` and `collections/menu` are gone from `src/style/override/_semantic.less`. Measured on the published artifact: `static/all.css` 374,894 → **307,785 bytes, −67,109 (−17.9%)**; at source level, before minification, 566 of 1,952 scoped rules and 152,239 of 602,156 bytes (−25.3%). Both numbers are recorded because they differ and the smaller one is the one a consumer actually downloads.

  **Nothing had to be re-homed, and that was checked from both ends rather than assumed.** From the markup: only `Listbox.js` and `Table.js` emit Semantic `ui` tokens at all, and the 38-example DOM baseline contains `ui popup` 0 times, `ui label` 0 and `ui menu` 0. From the CSS: of the 566 removed rules, the only ones naming a token still emitted are rooted at `.ui.menu` — an ancestor never rendered, since `Listbox` puts its `.menu` INSIDE `.ui.selection.dropdown` — or at `.ui.table td > .ui.ribbon.label`, a child never rendered.

  **Each module's reason, and one of them was a false comment that had protected 27% of the stylesheet.** `popup` was Semantic's tooltip, in-house since step 2 part 3. `label` was the multi-select chip, unreachable since `multiple` was removed at step 3 part 2 — the `modules/dropdown` import's "requires ... `label`" note turns out to describe Semantic's own multi-select MARKUP, not a compile dependency, measured by compiling without it. `menu` carried `// For Pagination Component`, which was simply untrue: `Pagination.js` emits `app__pagination*` and is styled by `src/style/components/pagination.less`. Verified in the browser after the deletion — the paginated table renders exactly as before.

  **The gate churn was two assertions and one inventory line**, which is the measurement that these modules were unused. `class-inventory.txt` lost exactly ONE of its ~1,000 entries (`pagination`, Semantic's `.ui.pagination.menu`), and `css.compilation.test.js`'s `.ui.popup` assertion inverted to a set of `not.toContain`s so the modules cannot come back silently. Two mistakes made on the way and recorded in that file: LESS keeps `/* … */` comments in its output, so asserting on raw compiled text matched the new explanatory comment itself; and `.ui.menu` is the wrong probe for `collections/menu`, because `modules/dropdown` carries three `.ui.menu` rules of its own. Our own dead `.ui.selection.dropdown.readonly .label` chip override went with `elements/label`.

  **Licensing:** the two remaining modules (`globals/reset`, `modules/dropdown`) carry their `/*!` Semantic banners into the compiled output, so attribution survives for what is still loaded. The full MIT obligation lands with the SECOND half, which vendors compiled Semantic CSS rather than importing it.

  **SECOND HALF — ~~IN PROGRESS~~ SHIPPED (2026-09-15): `globals/reset` + `modules/dropdown`, ~91 KB.** **Heading marker corrected 2026-09-17:** this still read "IN PROGRESS" while the paragraphs directly below it already recorded "THE CSS IS VENDORED — SHIPPED (2026-09-15)" and "THE DEPENDENCY IS GONE — STEP 4 COMPLETE (2026-09-15)". Re-verified 2026-09-17: `src/style/vendor/semantic-reset.less` and `semantic-dropdown.less` exist and `_semantic.less` imports them, `semantic-ui-less` is gone from `package.json` and from `node_modules`, and `npm run css:fixture:check` reports the fixture up to date at 285 rules. Only the word was stale. This is the L one. `Listbox.js` deliberately emits Semantic's token vocabulary and our own `input.less`/`form.less`/`tabs.less` layer overrides on `.ui.selection.dropdown`. H8 no longer blocks it (decided and shipped).

  **THE PARITY GATE EXISTS NOW, and building it first was the point.** Step 4 has always defined done as "pixel parity of extracted CSS vs current compiled output" and there was no mechanism for that sentence — no screenshot assertion exists anywhere in `e2e/`. `scripts/generate-semantic-css-fixture.js` + `src/style/__tests__/css.semantic-parity.test.js` + the fixture `semantic-contributed-css.txt` pin **285 rules** with their declarations in cascade order: everything the two remaining imports contribute. Vendor the CSS, run the gate, and green IS the parity evidence.

  Three things about it worth knowing. The rule set is DERIVED, not hand-listed — the generator compiles the stylesheet twice, once as-is and once with the imports commented out, and takes the difference, so the fixture cannot drift from what the imports really contribute. It compares the LESS output BEFORE prefixwrap, because the question is whether our CSS reproduces Semantic's and the scoping stage has its own gate next door. And it was ACCEPTED the way this repo accepts a gate: perturbing `@borderColor` in `site.variables` makes it fail at rule 49 naming the exact declaration (`box-shadow: … rgba(34,36,38,.15)` → `… #123456`), and restoring it makes it pass.

  A negative result worth recording alongside: perturbing `@fontSize` does NOT move these rules. That is a fact about which of our theme variables actually feed the Semantic modules, not a hole in the gate — the `@borderColor` acceptance is what proves the gate can see a change.

  **THE CSS IS VENDORED — SHIPPED (2026-09-15), and the published artifact is byte-identical.** `src/style/vendor/semantic-reset.less` and `semantic-dropdown.less` are generated by `scripts/vendor-semantic-css.js`; `_semantic.less` imports them instead of reaching into `node_modules`. Evidence, in two independent forms: the parity gate passes on all 285 rules, and `static/all.css` has the SAME sha256 (`dcdb0a40…`) built from master and from the branch. Attribution landed first, in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`.

  **FOUR TRAPS, each found by the gate rather than by reading — which is the argument for building it first.**
  1. `.loadUIOverrides()` is not just our hook. `theme.less` defines it as two optional imports, and the FIRST is the theme's own overrides — which for `globals/reset` is where normalize.css lives. Stripping the mixin produced a 7-rule reset with the normalize body gone.
  2. Redefining `@siteFolder` to exclude our overrides does nothing, **silently**: a LESS mixin resolves variables in the scope where it is DEFINED, and that mixin is defined in `theme.less`. Replaced by a LESS file manager — which must implement the SYNC path too, or LESS takes a different route through the whole compile and folds `calc()` differently.
  3. LESS re-evaluates what it parses, so vendored CSS is not inert. Semantic builds `calc(100% + 2px)` from escaped strings precisely so LESS leaves it alone; as a plain literal it folded to `calc(102%)`, and 100% + 2px is not 102%. The generator re-escapes `calc(...)` on the way out. The file must stay `.less`, because our `:extend` has to keep reaching these rules.
  4. Our `override/**.overrides` must be imported with `@import (multiple)`, the form `loadUIOverrides` itself used. With a plain `@import` the `.outline()` mixin's nested `.inverted &` block emitted twice, duplicating two rules exactly.

  **AND ONE TRAP IN THE GATE ITSELF, worth recording because it looked exactly like a product bug.** Two separate false alarms came from the measurement, not the CSS. The generator detects live imports by line shape; it did not recognise `@import (multiple) "..."`, so the baseline compile kept our overrides while the full compile also had them — 47 rules subtracted themselves away and the gate reported them MISSING FROM THE OUTPUT when they were only missing from the MEASUREMENT. Earlier, two of the four apparent "duplicates" turned out to occur twice on master as well; chasing them cost real time. A gate that measures by difference needs its difference to be exact, and both lessons are now comments in `generate-semantic-css-fixture.js`. **A third, found 2026-09-30, broke a DIFFERENT suite:** the measurement wrote the edited `_semantic.less` over the real file for the length of its compile and put it back after, so a parallel jest worker compiling the stylesheet in that window lost the vendored reset and dropdown. `css.compilation.test.js` failed on "Missing 1 classes: item" in 4 of 40 full runs, and never alone. The edit now reaches that one compile only, in memory, as a LESS preprocessor; the contribution is byte-identical (285 rules), the failure did not recur in 40 more full runs, and `css.semantic-parity.test.js` pins that the measurement writes nothing under `src/style`.

  **THE DEPENDENCY IS GONE — STEP 4 COMPLETE (2026-09-15).** `semantic-ui-less` is out of `devDependencies` and out of `package-lock.json`, and the published `static/all.css` still has the SAME sha256 (`dcdb0a40…`) it had before any of step 4 — the whole exit is invisible to a consumer's rendering.

  What went with it: **three** webpack `theme.config` aliases (this plan named two — `webpack.watch.config.mjs` was the third), the `node_modules` `lessOptions.paths` entries in the demo and library configs, `scripts/install-theme-config.js` and its three call sites, and **89 of the 97 files** under `src/style/override/`.

  **The deletion was decided by the import graph, not by reading.** `less.render` reports the files it actually read; run against `index.less` it showed 0 `semantic-ui-less` files and only 4 live files under `override/`. Eight are kept: those four plus the four inputs the vendoring compile reads (`globals/reset.variables`, `globals/site.variables`, `modules/dropdown.variables`, `theme.config`) — kept deliberately so `scripts/vendor-semantic-css.js`'s stated recipe (`npm i --no-save semantic-ui-less@2.5.0 && node scripts/…`) remains TRUE rather than becoming a note about git history. Vendored third-party code without a reproducible derivation is a dead end.

  **`npx jest` no longer mutates `node_modules`** — the gotcha CLAUDE.md carried for months. The jest `setupFiles` entry survives as a documented no-op.

  **The LESS 3 pin does NOT dissolve with the package**, which this plan predicted it would (R8, §9.8). Both anchors turn out to be ours, measured: `javascriptEnabled` is required by `_variables.less:23` and `less-plugin-functions` by `round()` at `_variables.less:264`. Unpinning LESS is separate work with two named targets.

  One thing CI caught that a local check had missed: `lint:css` globs `src/style/**/*.less`, so the vendored files entered it and reported 2,295 violations of conventions upstream never followed. `.stylelintignore` excludes `src/style/vendor/` — generated third-party CSS is not where style decisions are made. The local check had been `npm run lint:css | tail -1`, which shows output and hides the exit code; gates are verified by exit code now.

- **Step 5 — cleanup + release — DONE EXCEPT THE RELEASE ITSELF (2026-09-15).** The release is deliberately deferred; everything else this step named is discharged. `semantic-ui-react` went at step 3½ and `semantic-ui-less` at **step 4**, not here — this step's own text said it would drop the latter, and the work simply landed one step earlier.

  **THE BUNDLE DELTA — MEASURED, and the estimate it replaces was low by 6.7×.** This step asked to "record the bundle delta (−30 KB+ per the source's own estimates: 27 KB Dropdown + 4 KB Table + Popup)". Those estimates came from comments in the source and had never been checked. Measured by building the library at `c754daf5` — the commit before step 1, where all three components still wrapped SUIR — and comparing with master:

  | artifact | before | after | saved | gzip saved |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `dist/index.js` | 436,690 | 333,529 | **103,161** (−23.6%) | 33,486 |
  | `static/all.css` | 407,288 | 307,785 | **99,503** (−24.4%) | 10,316 |
  | **total** | 843,978 | 641,314 | **202,664 (−24.0%)** | **43,802** |

  Why the estimate was so far off: it counted only the JS weight of the three components and had no CSS term at all. Half the saving is stylesheet — 67 KB from deleting three unused Semantic modules at step 4, the rest from the reset and dropdown modules shrinking as they were vendored and scoped. The gzip column is the number that describes a real page load: **~44 KB less transferred, every time.**

  The baseline build also demonstrated what the exit removed, by failing: a clean `npm ci` at `c754daf5` could not build until `theme.config` was copied into `node_modules/semantic-ui-less/` by hand. That dependency is gone.

  **THE DOCUMENTATION HALF, discharged 2026-09-15.** The changelog and `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` were brought true rather than merely finished: three statements in the changelog were wrong, two of them written during this workstream — that the Semantic *styles* were still loaded (they were vendored at step 4), that the tooltip "has been in-house for two releases" (it has shipped in NONE — `git tag --contains` is empty for that commit), and stale tarball figures. The props page still carried a "Step 3 — `Dropdown`" section of unmet obligations that the same page discharged, and a live "forwarded to semantic-ui-react" outcome with its tier vocabulary. All corrected at the source, since that page is generated.

  Added while there, because a MINOR that removes published props has to be actionable: `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` and `docs/SUPPORTED-VIEWS.md` now ship IN the tarball (they were site-only), and the manifest gained `repository` and `bugs`. A consumer who installs the release can reach the record of what was removed without knowing a URL.

  **WHAT IS DEFERRED, deliberately: the release itself.** Both acts — `npm publish` and `npm run build && npm run deploy` — are the maintainers' call and are not taken here. `master` therefore still carries React 17+18 and the whole F1 exit unreleased, and the changelog's `### Unreleased` heading is accurate.

  **NOT part of step 5, and both since CLOSED (2026-09-15):** the §9.8 LESS-pin re-evaluation, whose trigger was "right after step 4" — done, the project is on LESS 4 with the published CSS byte-identical; and `watch-lib`'s divergence from the published build (`yalc-watch` is how a consumer validates a pre-release, so it had to be right before one is offered) — done, watch now runs the library config itself. Fixing watch surfaced one more thing worth recording, because it wasted an afternoon and would waste another: `CleanRootStatic` used to tap `watchRun` as well as `beforeRun`, so with a watcher running, **any** source write emptied the root `static/` payload for the length of a rebuild. `css.semantic-parity.test.js` writes `override/_semantic.less` by design, so it tripped this on every run and three tests in `css.pipeline.parity.test.js` failed on a file that had existed moments earlier — looking exactly like flakiness (3/3 with a watcher up, 0/3 without), and invisible from inside jest, since the deletion is in another process. The hook now taps `beforeRun` only. **SEMVER — DECIDED (2026-09-11): this ships as a MINOR.** The maintainers' call, and it is recorded here so the earlier reasoning is not mistaken for the decision. What the plan said before: Step 0 found no unsupported passthrough props in the wild, so on that evidence a minor — with the tier-2 decision as what could turn it major, since dropping a published propType is breaking even when nothing observed uses it. Steps 2 and 3 then did drop published propTypes (19 tooltip names, and `search`/`multiple`/`allowAdditions` with their companions), and the analysis in those steps argued for a major on that basis. The owners weighed it against the evidence that nothing in either corpus or the consumer-only record declares any of them, and chose minor. `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` still carries the full `Dropped` inventory per component, which is where a consumer looks regardless of the version number.

##### F1.3 Sequencing & effort

Run after Phase 2 (React 18) and Phase 3 (contract tests — they are the safety net). Steps 1–2 can proceed in parallel with Workstream A; Step 3 deserves dedicated focus. Every step is gated by the §9.5 behavioral layer **plus the mandatory visual/keyboard/a11y suite**, which **now exists** (`playwright.config.js` + `e2e/`, shipped by Step 2 part 2, gating CI job `browser`). Step 1 shipped before it, against the behavioural layer plus CSS reading, and is **not** retro-credited: its `.ui.table` padding contract stays verified by reading compiled CSS. Step 2 is what forced the suite into existence — the tooltip's whole purpose is positional, part 1 established that no jsdom suite can express it, and part 2 could not be called done on jest alone. Step 3 inherits the harness, the keyboard/a11y probes and a measured `Dropdown` starting state (`e2e/reference.js` → `DROPDOWN`), which is the expensive half of its matrix, but the matrix itself is still Step 3's work. Step 2 also demonstrated a sequencing improvement worth reusing at step 3 — **measure and gate in one PR, swap in the next** — because the measurement changed what the swap is for (see corrections 2 and 3 there). Total: **~4–7 weeks** spread across independently shippable releases — Step 0's audit left every per-step estimate unchanged (S / S–M / L), and Step 1 came in at the small end of its S, so the schedule stands; what Step 0 changed is *where* the effort sits in Steps 2 and 3 and what each step's definition of done includes. One cost Step 1 surfaced that the estimates do not carry: taking a wrapper in-house also means restructuring the generated prop page, because a component that imports nothing cannot stay in a list whose entries are asserted to import SUIR. Completing 5a — including Step 3½ — removes the React 19 external blocker (§8).

One measurement worth carrying into Step 4's estimate: the five loaded semantic-ui-less modules compile to roughly **45% of the final CSS payload**, dominated by `modules/dropdown` and `collections/menu`. Step 4 is re-homing that, not trimming an edge.

#### F2 — `moment`: keep it; native-replacement feasibility analysis

**Decision: `moment` stays a peer dependency — no dayjs migration.** The only peer change is the Phase 1 widening `~2.29.4` → `^2.29.4` (§3.2). It is externalized (webpack externals), so it costs the library bundle nothing and hosts already provide it. Below is the requested analysis of replacing it with a *native, zero-dependency* implementation — feasible, but parked behind a decision gate.

##### F2.1 Audited usage surface (small and bounded)

| Call site | moment API used | Native equivalent needed |
|---|---|---|
| `Text.js:43` | `moment(str).format(dateFormat)` — gated by `ISO_8601_COMPLETE_DATE` regex | ISO→parts local parsing + token **formatter** |
| `TextDateValue.js:8` | `moment(value).format(config.dateFormat \|\| 'DD/MM/YYYY')` | same |
| `InputDate.js:58–65` | `moment(value)` parse; `moment(str, dateFormat).format('YYYY-MM-DD')` normalize-on-change | token **parser** (parse-by-format) |
| `InputDate.js:10,88,94` | rc-picker with `generateConfig` from `rc-picker/lib/generate/moment`; input `format={[dateFormat, 'YYYY-MM-DD']}` | custom `GenerateConfig<Date>` |
| `time.js` (~~`formatTime`, `toHours`~~ — **DELETED 2026-09-17**) | ~~`moment(t).format(f)`~~ — none remain | ~~**no production callers** (referenced only by `time.test.js`); `formatDuration` there is already moment-free~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-17:** both functions were deleted on `h1-dead-weight`, together with `time.js`'s thereby-unused `import moment` and `import { FORMAT_TIME_FOR_HUMAN }` and the 12 tests that covered them. Consequence for this analysis: `moment` is now imported **nowhere** under `src/core/utils/` — the remaining `formatDuration` (and `.shortEnglish`) was already moment-free, so the whole utils layer is moment-free and only the four component/rc-picker rows above are left on this table |

**Contract constraints (corrected by the re-audit — less favorable than first assessed, §2.6-12):** moment **does** cross the public API. The project's own docs promise it (`README:12,18` — the API "accepts `moment` instances", with cross-copy `instanceof` caveats; `docs.md:24–25`); `InputDate` feeds any `props.value` into `moment(value)` (instances included); and rc-picker callbacks emit moment objects outward — `onSelect` (wired to `onCalendarChange`, `InputDate.js:96`) and every passthrough such as `disabledDate`/`cellRender` (`{...props}` at `InputDate.js:89`). Also contract: `dateFormat` **tokens** are moment syntax (note the `dateFormat` *prop* ~~is currently dead — §2.6-2 — only the context default applies until §9.4 fixes the wiring~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-17:** §9.4 repaired the wiring, as §9.4-C-config already records. `rules.js:240` declares `dateFormat: type.String`, `:339` destructures it and `:384` publishes it via `<ConfigOverride dateFormat={dateFormat} …>`; `providers/ConfigOverride.jsx` merges it into `ConfigContext`; `components/TextDateValue.js:21` reads `moment(value).format(dateFormat || config.dateFormat || 'DD/MM/YYYY')`, so an explicit prop wins over the context default. Pinned by `pages/main/__tests__/UIRender.config-channel.test.js:63`, "formats values with the dateFormat it was given, not the context default"). Consequence: a native adapter changes observable callback types and is a **breaking change** for hosts that use those callbacks or pass instances — not the drop-in swap the first draft assumed.

##### F2.2 What a native implementation requires

1. **Token formatter** (~100–150 lines): documented subset `YYYY/YY, MMMM/MMM/MM/M, DD/D, dddd/ddd, HH/H/hh/h, mm/m, ss/s, A/a` + literal/escape handling; month/weekday names from `Intl.DateTimeFormat`.
2. **Token parser** (~100–150 lines): the platform has **no parse-by-format facility** (`Intl` only formats) — strict parsing of the same subset must be hand-written.
3. **The classic pitfall to engineer around:** `new Date('2024-05-10')` parses as **UTC** midnight, `moment('2024-05-10')` as **local** midnight → a naive swap produces off-by-one-day dates in negative-UTC-offset timezones. All date-only strings must be parsed from parts into local time.
4. **rc-picker date engine — two options:** (a) **use the shipped `rc-picker/lib/generate/dateFns`** — a `GenerateConfig<Date>` operating on **native `Date`**, with `date-fns` as an optional peer (lock-verified); a few tree-shaken KB, and calendar-grid correctness (week starts, month boundaries) stays upstream's problem — recommended.
