## Appendix A — Inventory quick reference

- **Class components — 3 source files (re-measured 2026-10-02):** the engine's error boundary, `RenderClass` in `engine/Render.tsx` (React offers no hook for `componentDidCatch`), and the field wrappers that `modules/form/utils.tsx` (`asField`) and `modules/form/asInputDateField.tsx` build. **1 since 2026-10-06:** each wrapper's class is a plain `FieldInstance` that a memoised function component hosts, one per mounted field, as a document's classes are hosted (`InstanceClass` on the returned component); only the error boundary is left. The rest went to hooks (§9.2, §9.3 step 6), and a document's own classes extend `DocumentInstance`, not `React.Component`. The inventory as it stood: ~~21 source files (a wider lifecycle/component grep also matches 6 test files and 3 doc-comment-only hits): components pack (`Collapse`, `Tabs`, `Carousel`, `Counter`, `Expand`, `ProgressBar`, `ProgressSteps`, `InputNative`), pages (`rules.js`, `Data.js`, `mapper.js`, `components/Tabs.js`, `TableView.js`, `LocalDraftTableRow.js`), modules (`form/utils.js`, `form/views/AutoSave.js`, `form/inputs/ToggleField.js`, `form/asInputDateField.js`, `upload/views/Upload.js`), core (`ui-render/Render.js`), demo (`Examples.jsx`)~~.
- **Key module sizes (re-measured 2026-10-02 with `wc -l`):** `rules.tsx` 1063 · `mapper.tsx` 676 · `form/utils.tsx` 826 · `transforms.ts` 511 · `Render.tsx` 286. On 2026-09-17, as `.js`, they read `rules.js` 1308 · `mapper.js` 710 · `form/utils.js` 634 · `transforms.js` 458 · `Render.js` 223. **Figures corrected 2026-09-17:** the inventory read `rules.js` 1244 · `mapper.js` 700 · `form/utils.js` 624 · `transforms.js` 423 · `Render.js` 133, and every one of the five was understated — most sharply `Render.js`, which has grown from a 133-line dispatcher into a 223-line class carrying an error boundary (`componentDidCatch`, :92) and `MetaPathContext` (:12, :74, :162). That widens what Phase 6's "decompose the engine" covers, and the derived "1868 lines across two files" quoted in §9.3 is now 1942.
- **Docs that name the React range (re-checked 2026-10-02):** the peer list, the install commands and the support note in `README.md` and in `src/demo/markdowns/docs.md`, which both read `^16.14.0 || ^17.0.0 || ^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0`. The changelog's entries name the range as it was when each was written. ~~`src/demo/markdowns/docs.md:16,37,41` · `README.md` (plus a prose peer-deps note at `changelog.md:50`)~~ — the pointers as they stood.
- **SUIR JS surface — ~~3 of 54 pack files~~ → 0 (re-measured 2026-10-02).** The package is not installed, nothing in `src` imports it, and the lint rule rejects an import of it anywhere in `src`. The name survives in five pack files (`Dropdown`, `Listbox`, `Pagination`, `Table`, `Tooltip`), in explanatory comments and in the development warnings for dropped props, and in comments in two demo files. ~~`grep -rn 'semantic-ui-react' src/core/components/{Table,TooltipPop,Dropdown}.js` returns only explanatory comments (`Table.js:9,:96` · `TooltipPop.js:7,:12,:103,:111,:118,:171,:318` · `Dropdown.js:26,:69`) — no import in any of the three.~~ (2026-09-17) The inventory as it stood: `Table.js` (subcomponent users: `mapper.js:170`, `TableView.js`, `LocalDraftTableRow.js`, `ErrorTable.js`) · `TooltipPop.js` (`Render.Tooltip`, `mapper.js:44,483`) · `Dropdown.js` (`mapper.js:540–544`).
- **semantic-ui-less modules actually imported** (`src/style/override/_semantic.less`) — **superseded 2026-09-17: there are none; the package itself is gone (9f393959).** `_semantic.less` now imports `../vendor/semantic-reset` and `../vendor/semantic-dropdown`, each followed by our own overrides; `ls src/style/override/globals` = `reset.overrides`, `reset.variables`, `site.variables` and `ls src/style/override/modules` = `dropdown.overrides`, `dropdown.variables`. The label/menu/popup modules were measured unused and deleted at F1 step 4's first half (9a531871). The inventory as it stood: `globals/reset`, `elements/label` (chips), `collections/menu` (own Pagination), `modules/dropdown`, `modules/popup`; `transition` already replaced by an in-house `transition.less` — **that last clause is still true** (`@import 'transition'` closes `_semantic.less`).
- **moment call sites (re-measured 2026-10-02):** `Text.tsx:63` · `TextDateValue.tsx:30` · `InputDate.tsx:115–123` (+ rc-picker moment `generateConfig` at :10/:166); as `.js` they were ~~`Text.js:43` · `TextDateValue.js:8` · `InputDate.js:58–65` (+ :10/:88)~~ · ~~`time.js` `formatTime`/`toHours` (test-only, no production callers)~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-17 (H1, branch `h1-dead-weight`): both functions are DELETED**, together with the file's now-unused `import moment` and `import { FORMAT_TIME_FOR_HUMAN }` and their 12 tests; `formatDuration` (and `.shortEnglish`) stay. Do not go looking for the two functions — read the struck clause as the inventory it was. **Consequence for §9.7-F2:** the remaining moment surface is exactly the three components plus the rc-picker `generateConfig`, and **moment is now imported nowhere in `src/core/utils/`**.
- **Structure facts (§9.9):** `ui-*-pack` alias imports: **0** across `src` · ~~`tsconfig-paths-webpack-plugin`: referenced by no config~~ **REMOVED 2026-08-10 (00d67612, the Phase 0.5/0.9 sweep): it is not a devDependency at all any more — `grep -n 'tsconfig-paths' package.json` returns nothing (re-verified 2026-09-17)** · ~~orphan components ×12: `Avatar`, `Badge`, `Carousel`, `Collapse`, `ErrorContent`, `FloatNumber`, `ImageSwatch`, `MenuButton`, `Tags` + cluster `ErrorTable`/`Square`/pack-`TabList`~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-17 (H1, branch `h1-dead-weight`): all 12 files are DELETED from `src/core/components/`** — the count is now history, not a set of files anyone can open. `ErrorTable` could not go alone (`ErrorContent` imported it), so the pair went together. Only the **pack** `TabList` went: the engine `TabList` (`src/core/pages/main/components/TabList.js`, now `src/core/engine/components/TabList.tsx`) is untouched and mapper still uses it, which resolves **half** of the H6 duplicate. ~~Pack `Tabs.js` is still on disk, so **H6 is not closed**.~~ **The other half closed 2026-09-22:** the pack `Tabs` is now `StandaloneTabs`, which closes H6's `Tabs`/`TabList` item; ~~H6's other renames are still open (§9.9-H6).~~ **H6's other renames closed too: 2026-10-02 (#155) and 2026-10-05 (#156).** ~~Re-running the Appendix B orphan scan now reports no zero-count pack file except the `index.js` barrel, which is a scan artefact~~ **Re-run 2026-10-02, once it was made to read `.ts`/`.tsx`: no pack file reads 0 (Appendix B)** · ~~layering: engine⇄form-modules cycle (`form/utils.js:13–14` ⇄ `rules.js:3`) + `components/Text.js:4` → `../modules/variables`~~ **layering: both gone 2026-09-23.** #69 dissolved the cycle (§9.3 step 2) and #64 removed `Text`'s import of `modules` (H5); the layer lint rejects either import · ~~demo entries at `src/` root: `index.js`, `main.jsx`, `App.jsx`~~ **the demo entries are in `src/demo/` since H3 (#63, 2026-09-23).**
- **Public-surface facts (§2.6):** runtime entry `library/index.ts → main.tsx` (`AppProvider → AppWrapper → engine`) · `require(dist)` returns one callable function with no `.default`/`.UIRender`; declarations, generated from the source since §9.6-E4, use `export =`, retain named types through a merged namespace, and pass locked React 16/17/18/19 checks in interop and direct-CommonJS modes (`scripts/test-public-types.js`) · ~~`dateFormat` prop is still dead (`Render.js:45`)~~ **CLOSED (re-verified 2026-09-17): the config channel works.** `Render.js:55–59` drops the prop *deliberately* — the format is configuration and travels through `ConfigContext`, which `rules.js:384` feeds via `<ConfigOverride>`, and `TextDateValue.js:21` reads `dateFormat || config.dateFormat`; pinned by `UIRender.config-channel.test.js:63` and announced at `changelog.md:32` ("**The `dateFormat`, `currency` and `language` props now work.**"). The cited `Render.js:45` no longer holds the destructure either — it is at :59 · ~~fixed-id portal root (`AppWrapper.js:17`)~~ the popup root keeps its fixed `id="render-popup-root"`, but each document's popups portal into its own (`AppWrapper.tsx:31`, §9.3 step 3) · `prepack` checks version sync and rebuilds; ~~the measured tarball is 295 files / 7.25 MB unpacked / 2.53 MB packed~~ ~~Figures re-measured 2026-09-17: 299 files / 6.44 MB unpacked / 2.39 MB packed, inside the 330 / 8 MB / 3 MB budgets~~ **Re-measured 2026-10-02** through the repo's own gate (`scripts/check-package-budget.js`, i.e. `npm pack --dry-run --json --ignore-scripts`): **23 files / 3.29 MiB unpacked / 0.85 MiB packed**, inside the 28 / 4 MiB / 1 MiB budgets set when the flags stopped shipping (2026-10-01, §10) — assets shipped once in the root `static/` payload with `dist/static/*.css` as `@import` re-exports, source maps kept by decision, and CI enforcing both the budgets and a packed-tarball server-render smoke.

## Appendix B — Verification commands

```bash
# Legacy lifecycle inventory
rg -n "UNSAFE_|componentWill(Mount|ReceiveProps|Update)" src

# React-19-sensitive patterns
rg -n "\.defaultProps\s*=" src
rg -n "ReactDOM.render|findDOMNode|contextTypes|getChildContext" src

# SUIR isolation invariant (must return nothing)
rg -l "from 'semantic-ui-react'" src | grep -v "^src/core/components" 

# moment usage funnel check (§9.7-F2, if it reopens — target: only the dateAdapter module remains)
rg -n "from 'moment'" src

# SUIR exit progress (§9.7-F1 — target: all three return nothing)
#
# THE SECOND COMMAND USED TO BE `grep -v '^\s*//' … | grep '@import'`, and it was UNSATISFIABLE
# once the exit completed: it matched our own prose comments and the legitimate imports of the
# vendored CSS in `src/style/vendor/`. A gate that cannot pass is not a gate. What the step
# actually asserts is that nothing REACHES the packages — expressed directly below.
rg -n "from 'semantic-ui-react'" src
rg -n --glob '*.less' '@import[^;]*(semantic-ui-less|@\{libPath\})' src/style   # .less only: the invariant is about LESS imports, and prose in .js tests names the package legitimately
node -e "const p=require('./package.json'); const bad=['semantic-ui-react','semantic-ui-less'].filter(d=>(p.dependencies||{})[d]||(p.devDependencies||{})[d]); if(bad.length){console.log(bad.join(' '));process.exit(1)}"

# SUIR erosion guard + published prop surface (§9.7-F1 step 0)
# The eslint rule fails lint:js on an `import` of the package anywhere in src (since §9.7-F1 step 3½;
# it used to exempt src/core/components). The generated page's own scan additionally catches
# `require()` and `jest.mock()`, which the rule cannot see.
npm run lint:js
npm run docs:props:check

# Structure: orphan component scan (§9.9-H1 — count of importing files per component; 0 = orphan)
# Since H1 landed (2026-09-17) the 12 named orphans are DELETED, so this is a regression guard, not a
# to-delete list. It reads `.ts`/`.tsx` since 2026-10-02. Written for `*.js`, it had scanned no
# component since the TypeScript migration: zsh rejects the empty glob, and bash runs the loop once,
# on the literal pattern. Re-run 2026-10-02: no pack file reads 0, and a planted file with no
# importer reads 0. Two traps in reading its output, both measured 2026-10-02:
#   - `index` is an artefact both ways. The barrel is imported by directory path (`from '../components'`),
#     which the pattern cannot see, and the 5 it reads are other `index` files' importers, two of them
#     doc comments;
#   - the pattern ignores the directory, so a pack file shares its count with any same-named file
#     elsewhere: `files` reads 5, and two of the five import `modules/variables/files.ts`. (`Tabs` was
#     the example until H6 renamed the pack copy `StandaloneTabs`, 2026-09-22.) A count includes the
#     demo too: `StandaloneTabs` reads 1, and that is `src/demo/components/NavTabs.jsx`. Check the
#     importers, not just the count.
find src/core/components -maxdepth 1 -type f \( -name '*.ts' -o -name '*.tsx' \) | while read -r f; do
  n=$(basename "$f"); n=${n%.tsx}; n=${n%.ts}
  c=$(rg -l "/${n}['\"]" src --glob '!*.test.*' | grep -v -E "^src/core/components/${n}\.tsx?$" | wc -l)
  echo "$c $n"; done | sort -n

# Structure: alias usage must stay zero (or be formally reintroduced with jest/webpack parity)
rg "from 'ui-(react|modules|utils)-pack" src

# TypeScript (§9.6: the migration is complete and `allowJs` is off; both run in CI)
npm run typecheck            # tsc --noEmit over src
npm run typecheck:contract   # the contract agreement (src/library/contract.agreement.ts) on its own

# prop-types retirement (§9.6-E5, done 2026-10-01 — target: both return nothing)
rg -l "from 'prop-types'" src
# a dependency entry: the lint rule that bans the import names the package seven times, as "name": "prop-types"
rg -n '"prop-types":' package.json

# Peer sanity of resolved deps. semantic-ui-react left the list 2026-10-02: it is not in the lock any more,
# and the command threw on it.
node -e "const l=require('./package-lock.json');for(const k of ['node_modules/react-final-form','node_modules/rc-picker','node_modules/@testing-library/react'])console.log(k,JSON.stringify(l.packages[k].peerDependencies))"

# Local CI contour: every npm script .github/workflows/ci.yml runs (re-checked 2026-10-08). The job
# `verify` runs the first seventeen in this order; each leg below is a job of its own.
npm run lint:js
npm run lint:css
npm run typecheck
npm run typecheck:contract
npm run docs:views:check
npm run docs:props:check
npm run css:fixture:check
npm run build-css:check
npm run test:coverage
npm run build-lib
npm run test:css:built
npm run test:types:consumer
npm run test:pack:budget
npm run test:pack:consumer
npm run test:pack:peers
npm run test:env-flags
npm run build
npm run test:react16        # gating leg `react-16-floor`: whole suite on the 16.14 peer floor
npm run test:react17        # gating leg `react-17`: whole suite on 17.0.2
npm run test:react19        # gating leg `react-19`: whole suite on 19.3.0
npm run test:e2e            # gating leg `browser`: the Playwright suite

# Packaging gates (§0.7 — budgets, duplication, packed-tarball smoke; both need a built dist/)
npm run test:pack           # build-lib + both gates
npm run test:pack:budget    # file count, sizes, required paths, re-export stubs, duplicate assets
npm run test:pack:consumer  # pack, extract, resolve every CSS @import/url(), server-render in isolation
npm pack --dry-run          # raw manifest, when a budget failure needs eyeballing

# Security baselines (§2.6-13)
npm audit --omit=dev   # must stay at 0
npm audit              # 0 since 2026-09-30 (§10); a new finding is new debt to triage

# Final-CSS scoping gate (§9.9-H8 — expect 0 unscoped globals after the fix)
# Reads the root payload: dist/static/all.css is only an @import re-export since §0.7.
node -e "const c=require('fs').readFileSync('static/all.css','utf8');console.log((c.match(/(^|\})(html|body|\*)[,{ ]/g)||[]).length)"
```

## Appendix C — Consolidated verification checklist

Every check this plan depends on, in one place. ✅ = already verified during the audits behind this plan (evidence in §2 / Appendix A); ☐ = open, bound to a phase. An item is not "done" until its box is checked in a PR or CI.

### Already verified — archived audit snapshot (2026-07-21); re-verify before relying on any line

**Heading corrected 2026-09-17:** this section used to be headed "Already verified (audit facts — re-verify only if the code moves first)", which told a reader these facts needed no re-checking. Its own trigger has since fired and was never honoured — `git log --oneline --since=2026-07-21 -- src/core/components src/style` is **23 commits** — so the ✅ lines below are a dated record of what the audits found, not current fact. Re-verified 2026-09-17, at least six of them are now false: the **SUIR surface** line (no file in `src` imports `semantic-ui-react`, no wrappers remain, and neither `semantic-ui-react` nor `semantic-ui-less` appears in `package.json` at all); and inside the 2026-07-21 re-audit line, **"eslint fails (11 errors / 28 warnings)"** (`lint:js` now runs behind `--max-warnings 0` and gates CI — §0.9), **"tarball 579 files / 11.6 MB"** (remeasured **299 files / 6.7 MB** on 2026-09-15 — changelog, "Packaging"), **"published types ≠ runtime"** (closed at 0.6 — see the Phase 0 list below), **"`dateFormat` prop dead"** (closed 2026-08-27, §9.4) and **"built CSS ships unscoped `html`/`body`/`*`"** (closed 2026-09-15, §9.9-H8, commit `8d803008`; the H8 gate in Appendix B now measures **0**). The individual lines are left exactly as they stood, so the record of what the audit saw survives — read each one as of 2026-07-21.

- ✅ No legacy React APIs in `src`: string refs, legacy context, `findDOMNode`, `unstable_*`, `react-dom/test-utils`, `element.ref` reads — all still zero, re-measured 2026-09-17. **`e.persist()` corrected 2026-09-17:** this line used to list `e.persist()` among them, and stopped being true on 2026-08-20, when one deliberate call was *added* to fix a React 16 event-pooling bug — `persistEvent()` at `src/core/modules/upload/views/Upload.js:34`, called at `:100` and `:105`, covered by `Upload.interactions.test.js:98-111`. §2.4 and §5 already record it as the one intended call site; only this line was never updated. Read the struck half as the audit fact it was, not as current state

The ongoing-invariant line "SUIR isolation grep: **nothing at all**" below already states the stronger version
- ✅ moment surface: 3 components + rc-picker `generateConfig`; ~~`formatTime`/`toHours` have no production callers~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-17 (H1): both are DELETED from `src/core/utils/time.js`**, with the file's now-unused `import moment` and `import { FORMAT_TIME_FOR_HUMAN }` and their 12 tests (`formatDuration`/`.shortEnglish` stay). The first clause still holds — `Text.js`, `TextDateValue.js`, `InputDate.js` + the rc-picker `generateConfig` are now the *whole* surface — and the F2-relevant consequence is that **moment is imported nowhere in `src/core/utils/`**. An edit to `src/demo/pages/Examples.jsx` reached the open demo as a hot update, and so did reverting it. ### Phase 2 — React 18

- ✅ RTL 16.3.2, migration cost two test files, zero `act()` warnings
- ✅ `TooltipPop`/`Image` on default parameters — `defaultProps` survives in both files only inside the comment explaining the conversion
- ✅ Automatic-batching pass found no regression: unit suite runs under `createRoot` with batching in effect, and the browser pass covered the form flow, both submit branches, `addData`/`removeData` and the cascading Select (the most batching-sensitive path, since its reset calls `onChange` from an effect). `flushSync` was not needed
- ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-10-05: every registered `view` was worked through one by one, and a gate keeps it so.** Measured first: `FIELD.TYPE` registers 37 views, the 38 examples declare 29 of them, and they render 27 when they first paint, because `Counter` and `Tooltip` sit only in a tab of `all` that is closed at first paint. In Chromium, on a production build of the demo, all 38 examples rendered with no console error, no page error and no unknown-view placeholder, and a light drive raised none either: all 17 comboboxes opened and closed, 13 of the 51 text inputs typed into (at most three per example), and all 6 checkboxes toggled. The ten views the examples do not render at first paint were each loaded into the `/demo` sandbox and driven. Nine did their job (`Counter` was read only once its animation had landed on its end value). **`Upload` threw:** declared with neither `formats` nor a `fileType` that names a preset, it read the missing format list as a string for its hover hint, and the node showed the render-error diagnostic. So did `Input` with `type: "file"` declared the same way. Fixed: such an upload accepts any file and its hint names no formats, and the rerun in Chromium raised nothing. `src/demo/examples/__tests__/examples.view-coverage.test.js` is the gate. It records the `view` of every node the mapper resolves while the examples first paint, and renders each view they leave out from a declaration of its own, through the published entry and under StrictMode. Each must raise no console output and do the one thing it is for, and the gate fails when a registered view is covered by neither. **The item as it stood on 2026-09-17:** **The SUIR-on-18 half is VOID 2026-09-17 — the subject is gone, not the question answered (§9.7-F1 step 3½).** Re-measured 2026-09-17: `semantic-ui-react` is not installed, is in neither dependency block, and no file in `src` imports it; of the five risky views named below, three are now in-house (`Dropdown`→`Listbox`, `Popup`→`TooltipPop`, `Table`) and two (`rc-picker`, `Tabs`) never went through SUIR at all. ~~What stays 🔶 is only the residual this item shares with §6-Step7 — "every registered `view`" was never worked through one by one — which is now a question about OUR components and deserves its own item rather than living on as a SUIR leftover.~~ The item as it stood: SUIR 3.0.0-beta.2 on 18: the **risky interactive** views were smoked in a browser (Dropdown, Popup, rc-picker, Tabs, Table sorting/pagination/inline edit) and all 38 examples render in the automated corpus, but "every registered `view`" was never worked through one by one. After F1 step 1 the claim also narrowed: `Table` no longer goes through SUIR at all

- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** the removal was made REAL by two guards the browser leg found, neither of them findable in jsdom — clicking a `<button>` focuses it (so focus-open reopened the collision instantly), and a touch tap's compatibility mouse sequence leaves a sticky emulated hover (so the bubble appeared 500 ms after every tap). Suppressed via `onPointerDown` and `event.pointerType`, and pinned in **both** legs — in jsdom the pointer event has to be hand-built, because RTL falls back to `MouseEvent` and drops `pointerType` (delivering `null`), which is why a first attempt read as unassertable
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** a **second positioning defect** found and fixed with the component already correct — `.tooltip-host` is `inline-flex`, but a flex item is blockified to `flex` and stretched by `align-items: stretch`, so the host came out 1222 px wide around a 35 px button and the bubble sat 559 px away. `width: fit-content` fixes it; `width` only, deliberately. Every jsdom suite was green while the shipped tooltip was unusable
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** **finding 4 closed** — the four corner placements fixed in `tooltip.less` by writing the losing offset back per corner, plus a second, independent fix to the verdict, which required every corner to align to its host's *left* edge and so would have kept `top right` and `bottom right` red after the CSS was right. All four were genuinely broken in CSS; the assertion defect affected two of them. 8 of 8 placements now
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** 23 of the 38 browser tests were `[R]`/`[R->I]` facts about the wrapper and were **re-measured and rewritten**, not deleted — including one that was passing for the wrong reason (an "absence" assertion counting the semantic-ui-react portal selector, which matches nothing once the tooltip is in-house, so "focus does not open it" stayed green after focus started opening it)
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** gates — jsdom 160 suites / 2477 tests / 38 snapshots, Chrome 39/39, `docs:props:check` green with `TooltipPop` moved to `IN_HOUSE` (which required making the generator's `subcomponents` and shared-`Part` readers optional: the in-house shape had assumed a component family). One `-u`: the `.tooltip-host` wrapper is new markup at tooltip sites, 10 snapshot lines
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** an adversarial review AFTER the step was green found three real bugs no gate could see — `aria-describedby` assigned instead of appended (unlinking a form control's own validation message for as long as the tooltip is open), the pointer guard outliving its interaction (a `pointerdown` that moved no focus suppressed the next keyboard focus, i.e. the guard disabled the path the removal created), and a controlled `open={true}` never reporting its first close. Each fix is pinned by a test **verified to fail when the fix is reverted**, one at a time
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** the review was RE-RUN after fixing the panel's own bookkeeping (a finding whose skeptics all died was being filed as "refuted" with no reason, making 34 non-results indistinguishable from judgements). Second pass: **27 upheld, 17 refuted, 11 unverified** of 55 — and it was the most productive of the three, finding two more behaviour bugs, four wrong answers to a caller, six tests that gated nothing (four of them broken by the FIRST fix pass), and one inverted causal claim
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** the review was run a THIRD time and every candidate now has a verdict — **28 upheld, 27 refuted, 0 unverified** of 55, with all 171 agents completing. Two of the four findings I had verified myself were refuted on evidence better than mine, and one of my own characterisations was wrong: I had recorded the `pointer-events: none` selection loss as *predating* this step. It does not — `git show master:…tooltip.less` has no such declaration, so this branch introduced it. But it is not the regression I called it either: `.app__slider` already sets `user-select: none` (`slider.less:20`), so the Slider value text was never mouse-selectable, and the handle drags on `pointerdown`, so a press inside the value bubble used to MOVE the slider — the declaration is an improvement there. The `Upload` bubble is a single word. Verified all three claims directly
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** the two CSS pins the third pass argued about are fixed on the agreed FACTS rather than on the vote. `CORNER_AXIS_DECLARATIONS` pinned `top` alone while its own comment promised that deleting any declaration as "redundant" would fail there; it now covers `transform` too — the least obviously load-bearing one, because `.tooltip.left`/`.right` set their own `transform` at equal specificity and later in source order, so `transform: none` on a corner is what stops the bubble shifting a full width left and half its height up. Verified by deleting it: the compiled rule loses the property and the test fails naming it. And `.tooltip.show { animation-delay: 0s }` is now labelled a CASCADE LOSER — measured `0s` with and without the class, because `.fade-in`'s shorthand resets it — so the pin cannot be read as proof the declaration does anything
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** `behaviourContract` was stored for in-house components and rendered only for wrappers, so the tooltip's whole open/close/dismiss contract was missing from `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` — the one document that actually reaches a consumer. It renders now, and **SC 1.4.13's Hoverable clause is named there as a known non-conformance** instead of being left for a reader to derive: the bubble cannot be hovered, Dismissable and Persistent are met, and it is kept because the alternative measured worse
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3:** the accessibility trade is **recorded, not resolved** — 1.4.13 wants hovered content hoverable and this bubble cannot be, because `pointer-events: none` is what stops it swallowing the pointer and flickering; and a touch-only device now has no way to see a tooltip at all. Both are consequences of decisions the maintainers took, and both are written down in `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md`, `e2e/reference.js` and §9.7-F1 step 2 rather than left for a reader to discover
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3 — OBLIGATION 1: the 500 ms open delay survives.** MET — `TooltipPop.js` keeps it in JavaScript and three suites pin it. The inline component reveals through the CSS rule `*:hover > &` (`style/components/tooltip.less`), which has no delay, so a naive convergence drops it. It is a deliberate UX decision, stated in `TooltipPop.js` — "avoiding an accidental popup when the user is already familiar with the UI" — and pinned to the millisecond in three places (`[I] hover opens after our 500 ms delay` in `e2e/corpus.tooltip.pw.js`, `TooltipPop.behavior.test.js`, and the 499/500 boundary in `UIRender.overlay-behavior.test.js`). Measured: the simulated convergence fails all of them. Keep it in JavaScript, or `transition-delay` if the reveal stays CSS — either way it must remain gated
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3 — OBLIGATION 2: click-to-open survives.** DECIDED THE OTHER WAY and recorded: `on` was DROPPED on the maintainers' instruction, with focus-open added so a keyboard still reaches the content. Documented in `docs/SUPPORTED-PROPS.md` under `dropped.on` — not by omission. SUIR's `on: ['click', 'hover']` gives the tooltip a click path the CSS rule has no equivalent for. Measured as failing under the simulated convergence (`[R] click opens with no delay, a second click closes`, `e2e/harness.tooltip.pw.js`). Decide deliberately: reimplement, or drop it and say so in the changelog — but not by omission
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3 — OBLIGATION 3: Escape and click-outside still dismiss.** MET — both implemented in `TooltipPop.js` and pinned in jsdom and in Chrome. This is accessibility, not polish: a pointer-only dismissal path leaves keyboard users with no way to close the bubble. Both are pinned (`[I] a click outside closes it; Escape closes it`, and `[I] Escape closes an open bubble from an unrelated native input` — the latter is only answerable in a browser, since jsdom has no native focus semantics). Measured: both fail under the simulated convergence, because the inline component has no JavaScript at all
- ☑ **Step 2 part 3 — HAZARD: the bubble must not intercept pointer events.** HANDLED — `pointer-events: none` is in `tooltip.less` and `css.tooltip-contract.test.js` pins it, with the 1.4.13 consequence recorded rather than hidden. An inline bubble positioned over its trigger swallows the pointer, so the trigger cannot be hovered — Playwright reported `<span class="tooltip …"> intercepts pointer events` and retried until timeout. `pointer-events: none` is therefore a requirement of the inline shape. `tooltip.less:12` hints at the same hazard from the other direction (`display: none` is used because `visibility: hidden` "will trigger hover"), and the reason `Slider`/`Upload` never hit it is that their bubbles do not cover an active area. Adding it fixed exactly one of the five simulated failures, so it is necessary and not sufficient
- ☑ **Step 2 part 2 (NEW, from the browser leg):** `tooltip.less`'s corner placements FIXED — all eight placements land off their host, verified in Chrome. `tooltip.less`'s corner placements fixed, or the vocabulary explicitly restricted — `.tooltip.left`/`.tooltip.right` set `top: 50%` at the same specificity as `.tooltip.top`/`.tooltip.bottom`, so `top left`/`top right` land ON the host and `bottom left`/`bottom right` degrade to `left`/`right`. `top left` is the only placement `TooltipPop` uses, so converging on the in-house `Tooltip` without this ships a bubble sitting on its own trigger
- ☑ **Step 2 part 2:** all five discharged — `omitProps` applied (~~the boundary leaks `view`/`index`/`symbol` onto the bubble today, pinned as a defect~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-17 (S2 part 3)**, as §9.7-F1.2-S2-D1 already records and this row did not: `src/core/components/TooltipPop.js:503` spreads `{...omitProps(supported, ENGINE_PROPS, FIELD_ONLY_PROPS)}` onto the bubble, and `view`, `index` and `symbol` are the first, second and fifth entries of `ENGINE_PROPS` in `src/core/components/domProps.js`, whose "where the lists are applied" note lists `Tooltip (span)` — so none of the three reaches the bubble as an HTML attribute any more); `on` decided; the 45-prop passthrough decided; the `items` trigger shape decided; the dead 4-site `TooltipPop` chain in `modules/form/utils.js` deleted
- ☑ **Steps 2–3:** per component — behavioral contract layer + example QA green (full-DOM snapshots regenerated deliberately); emitted classNames keep existing LESS working
- ☑ **Step 3:** Dropdown keyboard/a11y matrix verified, incl. cascading-Select flows from `rules.js`. NOTE the deviation from this line as written: the control adopted the WAI-ARIA **listbox** pattern, not combobox — it stays a `role="listbox"`, and `e2e/reference.js` keeps the four remaining combobox attributes as an inventory rather than a debt. **The combobox pattern followed on 2026-10-05.** The `.text` is the `combobox` that takes focus, and the `.menu` the `listbox` BESIDE it. Beside, not inside, measured in Chromium's accessibility tree: with the dropdown itself as the combobox, the open list folded into its value ("Gold Gold" for a selected "Gold"). `aria-haspopup` is always set, `aria-controls` while the list is open, and a `label` names the control as `aria-label`. The inventory is wired, and `e2e/reference.js` records what is there instead. Every focus style moved with the focus to `:focus-within`, and each of the dropdown's interaction states computes the same border, shadow, background and radius as before. Hosts that find the trigger by `[role="listbox"]` must use `[role="combobox"]` (changelog)
- ☑ **Step 3:** replacement keeps `displayName = 'Dropdown'`, or the form-adapter branch (`form/utils.js:193,199`) is refactored in the same PR
- ☑ **Step 3½:** `semantic-ui-react` absent from `dependencies` (this is the 5a exit and the React 19 unblock)
- 🔶 Visual/keyboard/a11y suite green per step (mandatory, §9.5) — **the suite now EXISTS** (`playwright.config.js` + `e2e/`, gating CI job `browser`), shipped by Step 2 part 2 and **built for Step 2**. Green for Step 2: all ten named jsdom gaps closed except real assistive technology, which no browser runner can close. **Still NOT met for Step 1** and deliberately not retro-credited — its `.ui.table` padding contract was verified by reading the compiled CSS and by class-string assertions, not by rendered pixels, and stays that way. ~~**Not met for Step 3**, which owns its own `Dropdown` keyboard/a11y matrix;~~ **Met for Step 3** (R9, retired 2026-09-17): its own `Dropdown` keyboard/a11y matrix is verified (the Step 3 line above), in an `e2e/keyboard-a11y.pw.js` that ended up wider than the library's. What it inherited was the harness, the probes and a measured starting state (`e2e/reference.js` → `DROPDOWN`: role census, `aria-expanded` behaviour, options present when closed, and the five missing combobox ARIA attributes as a defect inventory, all five wired by 2026-10-05: see Step 3 above). ### F4 — form-stack major bump

Postponed 2026-09-30 (§9.7-F4), and no longer a React 19 prerequisite. **Postponed again 2026-10-07, after the audit below: until a `react-final-form` release fixes a regression that loses what the user entered.**

- ☑ `final-form` 4→5 and `react-final-form` 6→7 changelog audit against `rules.js`/`form/utils.js` usage (subscriptions, arrays, mutators) — **done 2026-10-07**, for all four packages, against `final-form` 5.0.1, `final-form-arrays` 4.0.1, `react-final-form` 7.0.1 and `react-final-form-arrays` 5.0.0: their release notes and diffs, and the suite run on them under React 16.14, 17, 18 and 19.
  - **The blocker.** `react-final-form` 7.0.1's pull request #1069 sets a field that mounts while final-form holds no state for its name back to its initial value. final-form drops a field's state when its last instance unmounts, so what the user entered is lost after a tab switch, on reopening a popup (the `rules.popup-content` failure §9.7-F4 had left undiagnosed), in a field that first mounts after its value changed, such as a review tab's, and in a table, whose FieldArray is reset the same way. Upstream tracks it as [issue #1095](https://github.com/final-form/react-final-form/issues/1095); the fix, [#1096](https://github.com/final-form/react-final-form/pull/1096), is open, and npm's `latest` is still 7.0.1. `addData` and `removeData` are not affected: they reset the form's initial values to its values.
  - **Under StrictMode,** `AutoSave`, and `AutoSubmit` through it, lose the first change on React 18 and 19. 7.0.1 calls a FormSpy's `onChange` once, from an effect (`react-final-form` #1076), and StrictMode's cleanup cancels that call, which is the one that sets AutoSave's baseline. Without StrictMode nothing changes.
  - **What else a host would see change:** a `null` value formatted `currency` or `percent` shows "0.00" or "0" instead of nothing; a toggle whose value comes from `defaultValue` renders unchecked on the server and in its first pass; removing a table's last row leaves `[]` in the data, where the key used to go (`final-form-arrays` #104); `onDataChanged` fires more often, because `final-form` 5 counts more forms dirty (`final-form` #528); every field renders twice at mount, so `translate` runs more (§9.7-F4 has the counts).
  - **And:** one type error, at the `setFieldTouched` mutator of `form/utils.tsx`; `submit()` returns a promise where it returned `undefined`, which nothing reads; and the form stack's own production build grows by 6.8 KB minified, 2.2 KB gzipped.
- ☑ **Guards, 2026-10-07.** Four tests pass on 6.5.9 and fail on 7.0.1: the three of `engine/__tests__/UIRender.remount.test.js` (a tab switch, a field that first mounts after an edit made elsewhere, a table's remount) and `AutoSave.test.js`'s StrictMode test, which passes on React 16 and 17, whose StrictMode runs each effect once. With them the suite fails 10 tests on 7.0.1 under React 18 and 19, and 8 under 16.14 and 17.
- ☐ All four packages bumped in **one PR**, on React 18, form-flow + contract suites green. **Waits for a `react-final-form` release with #1096.** If it cannot wait, measured 2026-10-07: a placeholder registration for each field and for a table's FieldArray, held from before the paint until the field's own registration has run, whenever the value differs from the initial one. The audit's first version registered only when final-form held no state for the name, so it missed a field that mounts in the commit that unmounts the last field of its name, the second guard. With the placeholder, the suite on 7.0.1 under React 18 fails 6 tests, and none of them is a field losing its value: the three render and `translate` counts, `inputToggle`'s snapshot, and the two StrictMode saves, which need AutoSave to take its baseline on FormSpy's first call rather than through the debounce.

- ☑ E5: semantic `type` proxy ~~recreated as TS aliases (same vocabulary) before the engine converts~~ **deleted instead, 2026-10-01:** 4 of its 41 names carried a meaning beyond PropTypes', and the props' JSDoc carries it (§9.6-E5 record)

- ☑ Go/no-go on the E2/E3 tail held after E1 — **DECIDED 2026-09-30: GO**, on the pilot's reading (§9.6, Governance), and the tail is done (§9.6, definition of done). **The velocity measurement now exists** (§9.6-E1: 20 files, 4142 lines, 364 agent-minutes, 23 `any` against 199 `unknown`); ~~the DECISION is still outstanding~~, and §9.6's governance note argues it should turn on a 2–3 component pilot rather than on extrapolating pure utils; hand-written d.ts hedged with type-level tests until E4

### Phase 6 — engine decomposition (§9.3)

- ☑ Module-global mutable state inventoried and moved per-instance (`Active.*`, `FIELD.FUNC`, `errorHandlerFunction`, `formsStorage`, `errorsMap`, `formInitialValues`, `storedTouched`, translation registries) — R14; portal root made per-instance — **DONE (re-checked 2026-10-01).** §9.3 step 3 closed each item or found it not to leak: `FIELD.FUNC` is snapshotted per render, ~~`formsStorage` stays shared on purpose~~ `formsStorage` is read per document tree since 2026-10-06 (R14 records the leak), and no instance writes the translation registries. R14 is retired on that evidence.
- ☑ Runtime hazards fixed (§9.3 step 4): subscribe-per-render + kept unsubscribe, setState-in-render, prototype-shared debounce, `set(this.state)` mutation, AutoSave/autoSubmit timer hygiene — **all five CLOSED by 2026-09-23**, and §9.3 step 4 records "This step is now empty"; R17 is retired.
- ☑ Engine⇄form-modules import cycle dissolved (§9.3 step 2) — **gone 2026-09-23.** Re-checked 2026-10-01: no source file under `src/core/modules` imports the engine; only its tests do, to render whole documents. No layer-rule suppression is left in `src`.
- ☑ Two simultaneous `UIRender` instances on one page covered by the contract suite — `rules.two-instances.test.js`, `rules.two-instances-errors.test.js`, `library/__tests__/popup-root.two-instances.test.js`, and the two-documents case of `examples.strict-mode.test.js`.
- ☑ Demo runs clean under `<StrictMode>` per the §7 definition (lifecycle warnings, subscriptions, cleanup, two-instance isolation) — **DONE 2026-09-29** (§9.3 step 7). `examples.strict-mode.test.js` pins it, on virtual time since #149.

### React 19 flip (§8 fast path — after 5a)

- ☑ **Settled by §10:** script-tag consumption unsupported (2026-08-20), ESM not now (2026-09-30). ~~**Host consumption mode confirmed with host teams (bundler vs script-tag)** — the only item requiring a human answer; it decides how much F3 (ESM) matters and settles the §10 script-tag gate~~
- ☑ `semantic-ui-react` absent from `dependencies` (F1 step 3½ / Phase 5a) — shipped; `npm prune` is part of it, or it stays resolvable locally
- ☑ `defaultProps` fixed (Phase 2) and automatic JSX runtime enabled (2026-09-30, §9.8)
- ☑ ~~`@testing-library/react` ≥16.1 in place — `^16.3.2` (the first RTL with `react ^19` peers); `react@19` in the dev/CI matrix; full regression green; peers widened additively (`^16.14 || ^17 || ^18 || ^19`)~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-17 — the ☑ was wrong; only the RTL clause of the four holds.** Re-measured 2026-09-17: ☑ RTL is `^16.3.2`, whose own peers read `react`/`react-dom` `^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0`. ☐ `react@19` is NOT in the dev/CI matrix in any gating sense — there is no `react@19` devDependency and no `jest.react19.config.js`, and the only React 19 leg is the `react-19-advisory` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, which carries `continue-on-error: true` by design. ☐ peers are NOT widened: `package.json` still reads `"react": "^16.14.0 || ^17.0.0 || ^18.0.0"` and the same for `react-dom` — which is exactly what §9.7-F1 step 3½ says above under "What it deliberately did NOT do: widen our own `peerDependencies`". React 19 is therefore not a supported target yet, and must not be described as one. **☑ **DONE 2026-09-30:** React 19.3.0 is a gating fixture leg (`npm run test:react19`, CI job `react-19`, which replaces `react-19-advisory` and needs no `--legacy-peer-deps`), the packed artifact is server-rendered on it (`test:pack:peers`), the published declarations compile against `@types/react` 19 (`test:types`), and the peers read `^16.14.0 || ^17.0.0 || ^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0`.**
- ☑ ~~`@types/react@19` pinned for `gen-ts`~~ not needed: `gen-ts` stays on the dev tree's 18, and `test:types` compiles the published declarations against `@types/react` 19 (2026-09-30)
- ☑ rc-picker / react-refresh / eslint-plugin-react-hooks behave on 19 in the matrix — rc-picker is bundled and runs on 19 in `test:react19`, the date inputs' suites included; react-refresh and eslint-plugin-react-hooks never run on 19, because the demo and the lint run on the dev tree's 18 (2026-09-30)
